- **Weibull:** at this stage the estimators of the live tool were connected through an adapter (maximum likelihood and moments with fixed a; moment variance still n − 1, written in the result as a warning; the n-divisor default in P05). Changed in P05, see below.
- **Goodness-of-fit measures (family-independent):** ln L = Σ logpdf; KS D = max(D⁺, D⁻), D⁺ = max[j/n − F(d₍ⱼ₎)], D⁻ = max[F(d₍ⱼ₎) − (j − 1)/n]; AD A² from logcdf and logsf; AIC = 2k − 2ℓ, BIC = k ln n − 2ℓ only for methods that maximise the likelihood (k = number of free parameters). No p-value.
- **Shared numerical core:** in `core/stats.js` the normal distribution tail is computed directly with the upper incomplete gamma (Φ(−z) = ½·Q(½, z²/2), |z| ≳ 1.73); `normSf`, `logNormCdf`, `logNormSf`, `gammaQ` were added. The old version was 2% off at Φ(−8) and gave Φ(−10) = 0; now within relative 2·10⁻¹³ of SciPy, ln Φ(−40) finite. The tests of the height–diameter and sampling tools passed unchanged. `core/roots.js`: Brent's method (with status reporting: ok, no sign change, non-finite value, iteration limit).
- **Validation:** `tests/ref/make_dist_p01.py` (SciPy): Weibull functions on a grid extending into both tails (relative 10⁻¹¹), normal tails, Q(a, x), roots; on the examples of the live tool, maximum likelihood **by direct minimisation of −ln L** (not by the score equation that the JS solves), moments with brentq, KS and A². `tests/dist/test_p01.mjs` 870/870: agreement with SciPy, parameters identical to the live tool, specification §8.1 Weibull row, registry (nothing unimplemented is offered; a new family is added only through registration), validation errors, data contract. `tests/dist/e2e.mjs` 33/33.


### P05: Weibull direct estimation (10 Nov 2026)
Compared with specification §3.5 and §3.6. The names were already standard (a = location, b = scale, c = shape; F(d) = 1 − exp(−((d − a)/b)^c), d > a); no external library is used, SciPy's `weibull_min(c, loc=a, scale=b)` mapping is only in the reference scripts. The location **is not estimated by any method**: a = 0 (two-parameter form, always computed) or an a fixed by the user (a ≥ 0, a < smallest diameter; a 0 typed by the user is recorded with origin "user"). A free-location (three-parameter) method was not added.

The estimators were moved into the engine (`dist/fit/weibull.js`; it no longer imports `weibull/calc.js`, that file goes to `legacy/` in 2.0.0). The numerical solver is recorded separately from the method:

| Method (id) | Estimation principle | Equation | Solver |
|---|---|---|---|
| `weibull.ml` | maximum likelihood, a fixed | x = d − a; Σxᶜ ln x / Σxᶜ − 1/c − mean(ln x) = 0; b = (mean xᶜ)^(1/c) | geometric bisection, c ∈ [0.01, 1000], relative width 10⁻¹⁴ |
| `weibull.mom` | moments (mean, variance), a fixed | Γ(1 + 2/c)/Γ²(1 + 1/c) − 1 = v/x̄²; b = x̄/Γ(1 + 1/c) | same |
| `weibull.pct` | two percentiles, a fixed | A_q = −ln(1 − q); c = ln(A_r/A_p) / ln[(Q_r − a)/(Q_p − a)]; b = (Q_p − a)/A_p^(1/c) | closed form |

- **Variance divisor (decision 2):** default v = Σ(x − x̄)²/n; option s² = Σ(x − x̄)²/(n − 1). The only difference between them is the factor (n − 1)/n in v; the n − 1 option reproduces the results of 1.0.0 bit for bit (same equation, same solver, same stopping rule). With the n divisor v is smaller, so c is larger. The divisor used is written in the result record (`settings.varDivisor`, `targets.varDivisor`) and on screen.
- **Percentiles:** sample percentiles Hyndman and Fan (1996) type 7 (R default, numpy `linear`; Hyndman, R.J., Fan, Y. 1996. Sample quantiles in statistical packages. *The American Statistician* 50(4): 361–365. **[B]** (PDF provided by the user, read on 22 Nov 2026: Definition 7, p_k = (k − 1)/(n − 1) (Gumbel 1939), in form (1) with m = 1 − p); the definition was also verified numerically with numpy): h = (n − 1)p, Q = x₍⌊h⌋+1₎ + (h − ⌊h⌋)(x₍⌊h⌋+2₎ − x₍⌊h⌋+1₎). Default pair p = 0.24, r = 0.93: Dubey (1967) **[B]** (note in §2; the most efficient pair for joint estimation of scale and shape, rounded). The user may type another pair with 0 < p < r < 1. If Q_r = Q_p (tied data) the shape is undefined: the result is "not computed", the two percentiles are written to the record, no number is produced. Q_p > a follows automatically from the condition a < smallest diameter. Since the method does not maximise the likelihood, AIC/BIC are not given; ln L is given for information.
- **Result contract** (`dist-result/1`, two backward-compatible fields added): `settings` (the method options used) and `targets` (the sample summaries matched by the method and their definitions: mean and variance, or Q_p and Q_r). Method options are defined in the registry (`options`: `choice`, `prob`), their checking belongs to the method (`checkOptions`); an invalid option gives the status "not computed".
- **Compatibility:** method ids did not change (`weibull.ml`, `weibull.mom`); old sessions of the development page open with the defaults of the new options, no conversion is needed. 1.0.0 sessions are not carried over to the new tool (P00 §9).
- **Validation:** `tests/ref/make_dist_p05.py` (SciPy 1.18.1, NumPy 2.5.3) uses none of the closed forms or the univariate reduction of the JS: moments by the joint `least_squares` solution of the two equations (`weibull_min.stats`), percentiles by the joint solution of the equations `weibull_min.ppf(p) = Q_p`, `ppf(r) = Q_r`, maximum likelihood both by `weibull_min.fit(floc=a)` and by minimising −ln L with Nelder–Mead; sample percentiles `numpy.quantile(method='linear')`. Examples: `tests/weibull/fixture.json` and four simulated samples generated with `default_rng(20261110)` (not real measurements), each with a = 0 and a = min/2 (one also with the true a = 5), five percentile pairs. `tests/dist/test_p05.mjs` 1376/1376: moments and percentiles relative 10⁻¹⁰ to SciPy, maximum likelihood 2·10⁻⁶ (the precision of the SciPy minimisers themselves; ln L in JS is in every case equal to or larger than SciPy's), the mean/variance and Q(p)/Q(r) of the fitted distribution equal to the targets, that the default n divisor is the same as the 1.0.0 equation with v = s²(n − 1)/n, bit-level identity with 1.0.0 (maximum likelihood; moments with n − 1 divisor; statuses), specification §8.2 (recovery of c and b within 10⁻¹² from the theoretical percentiles of a = 5, b = 20, c = 2.5 with four pairs), invalid p/r, tied percentiles, the limit of the search interval. Browser test `tests/dist/e2e.mjs` 38/38.

### P02: normal and two-parameter lognormal (10 Nov 2026)
Specification §3.1–3.2. Sources: [R10] and [R11] in the specification (NIST/SEMATECH e-Handbook §1.3.6.6.1 normal, §1.3.6.6.9 lognormal; read on 22 Nov 2026) **[B]**: the normal density and the two-parameter lognormal (θ = 0, μ = ln m) agree with the definitions here; the equations were also verified independently with SciPy (`scipy.stats.norm`, `scipy.stats.lognorm(s = σ, scale = e^μ)`).

**Families** (`dist/families/normal.js`, `lognormal.js`):
- Normal: μ (cm), σ > 0 (cm); support all real numbers. For every fitted normal, the probability of a negative diameter **Φ(−μ/σ)** is written to the result record (`diagnostics.pBelowZero`) and on screen (small values in exponential notation, not rounded to 0).
- Lognormal (unshifted 2P): ln D ~ N(μ, σ²). **μ and σ are on the log scale**: μ = E[ln D] (unit ln cm), not the arithmetic mean in cm; σ is unitless. On screen "μ (ln cm)", "σ (log scale)". Support d > 0. The density is on the diameter scale: f(d) = φ((ln d − μ)/σ)/(σd); ln L contains the Jacobian −Σ ln d, so AIC/BIC are comparable on the same scale as Weibull and normal. E[D] = exp(μ + σ²/2), Var[D] = (e^{σ²} − 1)e^{2μ + σ²} (with expm1). There is no three-parameter (shifted) form; if added it becomes a separate family/method.
- `core/stats.js` `normQuantile`: bisection in the lower tail, for p > ½ Φ⁻¹(p) = −Φ⁻¹(1 − p) (for p ≥ ½, 1 − p is exact in floating point). The old version computed 1 − p for p < ½; relative precision was lost at very small p such as p = 10⁻¹². Now within relative 10⁻¹³ of SciPy in the range 10⁻³⁰⁰ ≤ p ≤ 1 − 10⁻¹⁵; height–diameter, sampling and all other tests passed unchanged.

**Direct estimation** (`dist/fit/normal.js`, closed form, no iteration):

| Method | Equation | Note |
|---|---|---|
| `normal.ml` | μ = m, σ² = v = Σ(d − m)²/n | The method of moments gives the same equations (the first two moments of the normal are μ and σ²); so that two identical rows are not shown, a single method is registered, on screen under the name "Maximum likelihood (= moments)" and with its explanation. The s² with n − 1 divisor is neither the maximum likelihood nor the moment estimate, and is not offered. |
| `lognormal.ml` | μ = mean(ln d), σ² = Σ(ln d − μ)²/n | maximum likelihood estimate of ln D; likelihood on the diameter scale |
| `lognormal.mom` | σ² = ln(1 + v/m²), μ = ln m − σ²/2 | v default n divisor, n − 1 option (as for Weibull; written in the result) |

Validation: n < 2, all diameters equal (zero variance) → "not computed"; in the lognormal, a zero or negative diameter → `d.nonpos` (count and example), no number is produced. The shared sample checks were moved to `dist/fit/common.js` (Weibull results did not change). **A measurement threshold is not supported** (P11): all methods assume untruncated data; this is stated explicitly on the settings screen, and there is no option to declare a threshold.

**Recovery from summaries** (`dist/recover/moments.js`; method kind `recover`, data type `summary`; a separate workflow in the interface, "Recovery from summaries"): input is the mean diameter m and **either** the variance **or** Dq; optionally the number of trees n. All are converted to the moment variance: v as is if it has the n divisor, if it is s² with the n − 1 divisor then v = s²(n − 1)/n (in this case n is required), if Dq is given v = Dq² − m² (Dq must be > m). Normal: μ = m, σ = √v; lognormal: σ² = ln(1 + v/m²), μ = ln m − σ²/2. The record contains the inputs (`targets`: m, v, Dq, source, divisor, n) and the back-calculated mean, Dq and moment variance (`diagnostics`); these must equal the entered values (a check; not a goodness-of-fit measure). Since there are no raw data, there is no ln L, AIC or goodness-of-fit measure. Invalid input (mean ≤ 0, variance ≤ 0, Dq ≤ m, both or neither, n missing with the n − 1 divisor, n not an integer or < 2) gives the status "not computed" and the reason is written.

**Result contract:** the field `diagnostics` was added (backward compatible). An optional `label` (μ, σ) and `scale: 'log'` were added to the family's `params` entry.

**Validation:** `tests/ref/make_dist_p02.py` (SciPy 1.18.1): the functions of both families on grids extending into both tails; normal maximum likelihood `norm.fit`; lognormal maximum likelihood both by minimising −Σ `lognorm.logpdf` with Nelder–Mead (diameter scale, Jacobian included) and by `lognorm.fit(floc=0)`; lognormal moments and all recoveries by jointly matching the distribution's mean and E[D²] to the targets with `least_squares` (the closed forms of the JS are not used). Examples: `tests/weibull/fixture.json` and three simulations generated with `default_rng(20261111)` (not real measurements); in recovery, five cases including the moments of specification §8.2. `tests/dist/test_p02.mjs` 931/931: functions 2·10⁻¹² (relative), Φ⁻¹ 10⁻¹³, specification §8.1 normal and lognormal rows, normal maximum likelihood 10⁻¹², lognormal maximum likelihood 10⁻⁷ (the precision of Nelder–Mead; the JS ln L is in every case equal to or larger than SciPy's), the Jacobian identity, moments 10⁻¹¹ and equality of the fitted mean/variance with the targets, reproduction of the given mean and Dq within 10⁻¹³ in recovery, all validation errors. `tests/dist/e2e.mjs` 53/53.

### P03: two-parameter gamma (10 Nov 2026)
Specification §3.3. Sources: [R12] (NIST e-Handbook §1.3.6.6.11, gamma; read on 22 Nov 2026) **[B]**: density with location 0 and scale θ, CDF via the lower incomplete gamma; [R13] (lmomco) **[D]**, not read; the equations were verified independently with SciPy. Minka, T.P. 2002. Estimating a gamma distribution (technical note) **[D]**: used only for the starting value of the solver, does not affect the result.

**Family** (`dist/families/gamma.js`): shape k > 0 (unitless), **scale** θ > 0 (cm); f(d) = d^(k−1)e^(−d/θ)/(Γ(k)θ^k), d > 0; F(d) = P(k, d/θ); mean kθ, variance kθ². For programs that use a rate, β = 1/θ (1/cm); in the SciPy reference `gamma(a = k, scale = θ)` was used explicitly with the scale. No location parameter; a free-location gamma is a separate model and is not offered. ln F and ln(1 − F) are computed directly (`core/stats.js` `lnGammaP`, `lnGammaQ`); the quantile by the inverse incomplete gamma (`gammaPInv`: Brent on t = ln x, ln P in the lower half, ln Q in the upper half; a quantile below the double-precision range is 0).

**Numerical core** (`core/special.js`): `lnMinusDigamma(x)` = ln x − ψ(x) is computed directly (asymptotic series for x ≥ 10, below that the recurrence ψ(x) = ψ(x + 1) − 1/x); there is no cancellation loss at large k in the difference ln x − digamma(x). `digamma(x)` is derived from it. 3·10⁻¹⁴ against SciPy `digamma`, 10⁻¹² against `gammaincinv` (relative).

| Method | Equation | Solver |
|---|---|---|
| `gamma.ml` | s = ln m − mean(ln d) > 0; ln k − ψ(k) = s (the left side is strictly decreasing in k, single root); θ = m/k | Brent, on t = ln k, absolute 10⁻¹⁵; the interval is expanded from the starting value (Minka), k ∈ [10⁻⁴, 10⁸] |
| `gamma.mom` | k = m²/v, θ = v/m; v n divisor (default) or n − 1 | closed form |
| `gamma.rec` (from summaries) | k = m²/v, θ = v/m; v is converted to the moment variance as in P02 | closed form |

**Special cases:** zero/negative diameter (`d.nonpos`), n < 2 and zero variance → "not computed"; s not positive in floating point although the diameters differ, or k > 10⁸ → "at interval boundary" (`k.overflow`, shape overflow); k < 10⁻⁴ → `k.boundary`; Brent fails or ln L not finite → "did not converge" (`k.noconverge`). In none of them are parameters given; **when maximum likelihood fails, the moment estimate is not returned under that label** (the test checks that the moment estimate exists but is not put into the ML row).

**Accuracy limit:** at very large k (diameters nearly equal; e.g. k ≈ 10⁴) lnΓ(k) ≈ 8·10⁴, so ln L can only be computed with about 10⁻⁹ absolute precision; the likelihood surface is very flat in this region (a 0.001% change in k changes ln L by 10⁻⁹).

**Validation:** `tests/ref/make_dist_p03.py` (SciPy 1.18.1): functions on grids k = 0.3 … 900 out to both tails; maximum likelihood both by `gamma.fit(floc = 0)` and by Nelder–Mead on (ln k, ln θ); moments and recovery with `gamma.stats` and `least_squares`. Examples: `tests/weibull/fixture.json` and three simulations with `default_rng(20261112)` (one a narrow distribution giving k ≈ 9645; not real measurements). `tests/dist/test_p03.mjs` 653/653: specification §8.1 gamma row; ML 10⁻⁸ against `gamma.fit`, 2·10⁻⁶ against Nelder–Mead (10⁻⁵ in the narrow sample), the JS ln L not smaller than the ln L computed in JS at the SciPy estimates, kθ = sample mean, score ≈ 0; moments 10⁻¹¹; in recovery mean and Dq within 10⁻¹³; all special cases. `tests/dist/e2e.mjs` 55/55.

### P04: location–scale beta and boundary policy (10 Nov 2026)
Specification §2.2, §3.4. Source: [R14] (NIST e-Handbook §1.3.6.6.17, beta with bounds a, b; read on 22 Nov 2026) **[B]**: with α = p, β = q, y = (d − L)/(U − L) the page's density is the one here; the equations were also verified independently with SciPy.

**Family** (`dist/families/beta.js`): Y = (D − L)/(U − L) ~ Beta(α, β), L < D < U; L, U (cm) support bounds, α, β > 0 shape (unitless). f(d) = y^(α−1)(1 − y)^(β−1)/(B(α, β)S), S = U − L. 1 − y is everywhere computed as (U − d)/S (the difference 1 − y is not taken), so the precision of the upper tail is preserved. ln F and ln(1 − F) directly with `core/stats.js` `lnBetaInc`; the quantile with `betaIncInvLower` (Brent on ln y), for q > ½ solved from the top: d = U − S·z, I_z(β, α) = 1 − q. The family's `check(p)` hook applies the rule L < U (added to the registry: cross-parameter rules).

**Boundary policy:** L and U are always fixed values entered by the user (`fixed`: origin `user`, `settings.boundPolicy = 'user'`). They are not estimated; the sample minimum/maximum is not made an automatic bound; **there is no automatic suggestion** (there is no universal rule in the literature, specification §2.2). On screen the smallest and largest diameter of the data are shown only for information, with the note "not a bound". Observations outside the bounds (`bound.outside`) and observations exactly on a bound (`bound.on`; the support is an open interval) are reported with count and example, and no estimation is done for that unit; observations are never shifted (no epsilon/jitter). Since the beta methods cannot run without bounds being entered, they are not selected by default in the settings. Free L/U estimation and GB1/GB2 are out of scope; the same model is not registered a second time under the name "generalised beta".

| Method | Equation | Solver |
|---|---|---|
| `beta.mom` | μ_y = (m − L)/S, v_y = v/S², t = μ_y(1 − μ_y)/v_y − 1, α = μ_y t, β = (1 − μ_y)t; v n divisor (default) or n − 1 | closed form |
| `beta.ml` | Σ[(α − 1)ln y + (β − 1)ln(1 − y)] − n ln B(α, β) − n ln S is maximised (−n ln S is the term for the change to the diameter scale) | Newton, exact (trigamma) Hessian, step halving; starts from the moments (if not admissible, from α = β = 1); score < 10⁻¹⁴ |

Moment admissibility: 0 < μ_y < 1 (`beta.mean`) and 0 < v_y < μ_y(1 − μ_y) (`beta.var`); if not satisfied the result is "not computed". While the data lie within [L, U], the variance with the n divisor cannot exceed this bound (Bhatia–Davis inequality), the s² with the n − 1 divisor can (in the test d = 1, 9; L = 0, U = 10). The log-likelihood is concave in (α, β) (exponential family), so Newton goes to the single maximum; if a shape exceeds 10⁸ "at interval boundary", if it does not converge "did not converge".

**Numerical core:** `core/special.js` `trigamma` (asymptotic series at x ≥ 20, recurrence below; 10⁻¹³ against SciPy `polygamma(1, ·)`); `core/stats.js` `lnBetaInc`, `betaIncInvLower`.

**Validation:** `tests/ref/make_dist_p04.py` (SciPy 1.18.1): functions for six parameter sets, at points approaching both ends to 10⁻¹². Because SciPy itself computes 1 − (x − L)/S at points near U, it loses digits; at these points the reference calls SciPy with the reflected Beta(β, α) at z = (U − x)/S (an identity; not the JS formula). Extreme log values that SciPy overflows to −∞ are written as `null` and in JS are checked to be finite and < −700. Maximum likelihood by `beta.fit(floc = L, fscale = S)` and Nelder–Mead on (ln α, ln β); moments with `beta.stats` and `least_squares`. Examples: `tests/weibull/fixture.json` (with bounds given explicitly in the test; these are not a rule for real data) and three simulations with `default_rng(20261113)`. `tests/dist/test_p04.mjs` 642/642: specification §8.1 beta row; ML 2·10⁻⁷ against `beta.fit`, 2·10⁻⁶ against Nelder–Mead (2·10⁻⁵ at large shape), the JS ln L not smaller than at the SciPy estimates, the identity ln L(d) = ln L(y) − n ln S; moments 10⁻¹¹; recovery of α, β from the moments of known α, β, L, U (8 cases, 10⁻¹¹); inadmissible bound/moment combinations, missing/reversed/negative bounds, observations outside and on the bounds (data unchanged), rejection when the sample min/max is given as a bound; α < 1 and U-shaped samples. `tests/dist/e2e.mjs` 62/62.

### P08: Johnson SB, direct fit with fixed ξ and λ (10 Nov 2026)
Specification §3.7. Sources: ForestFit `fitJSB` documentation ([R7]) **[D]**, not read; Knoebel, B.R., Burkhart, H.E. 1991. A bivariate distribution approach to modeling forest diameter distributions at two points in time. *Biometrics* 47: 241–253. **[B]** (PDF provided by the user, read on 22 Nov 2026: §3.1, with ξ and λ given, the 50th and 95th percentiles give γ = −δ log[(D50 − ξ)/(ξ + λ − D50)] and δ from z₉₅; the same as `johnsonsb.kb` below; earlier note: reference written from memory, not read; cited in the specification as the source of the 50/95 percentile pair). The equations were verified independently with SciPy and mpmath.

**Family** (`dist/families/johnsonsb.js`): ξ lower bound (cm), λ > 0 range (cm), support ξ < d < ξ + λ; γ (real) and δ > 0 shape. z = γ + δ ln[(d − ξ)/(ξ + λ − d)], F = Φ(z), f = φ(z)δλ/[(d − ξ)(ξ + λ − d)]; ln F, ln(1 − F) with `logNormCdf`/`logNormSf`; quantile ξ + λ·logistic(w), w = (Φ⁻¹(p) − γ)/δ, written from the top in the upper half (ξ + λ − λ·logistic(−w)); ξ + λ − d is computed directly. At very small δ the distance of an extreme quantile to the bound can be smaller than the spacing of double-precision numbers at that point; the correctly rounded quantile is then the bound itself (checked separately in the test). The moments have no closed form: for Y = logistic((Z − γ)/δ), Z ~ N(0, 1), E[Y] and Var[Y] by **composite Gauss–Legendre** (`core/quad.js`: |z| ≤ 10, panels of width 0.5, 10 nodes per panel, breakpoints z = γ ± kδ, k = ¼ … 16). The variance is computed centred around the median of Y, c = logistic(−γ/δ) (E[(Y − c)²] − E[Y − c]²); the digit loss in the difference m₂ − m₁² at large δ is thus avoided. Against mpmath (40 digits) E[Y] within 2·10⁻¹⁴, Var[Y] within 5·10⁻¹³. SciPy's `johnsonsb.stats` value is not at this accuracy (~10⁻⁸ to 10⁻¹⁰) and was not used as a reference.

**Boundary policy:** same as for beta (P04): ξ and λ are fixed values entered by the user; they are not estimated, not taken automatically from the data or from program constants, there is no suggestion; observations outside and on the bounds are reported separately and not shifted. Only γ and δ are estimated; **no method is a joint estimation of the four parameters** and none is named as such. In the settings the bounds card appears according to the family ("Johnson SB bounds ξ and λ"; the upper bound ξ + λ is shown); SB methods are not selected by default.

| Method | Equation | Solver |
|---|---|---|
| `johnsonsb.cml` | t = ln[(d − ξ)/(ξ + λ − d)]; δ = 1/s_t, γ = −t̄/s_t, s_t² **with n divisor** | closed form (maximum likelihood estimate of γ, δ given ξ, λ; AIC/BIC k = 2) |
| `johnsonsb.kb` | fixed pair p = 0.50, r = 0.95: t_q = logit[(Q_q − ξ)/λ], z_q = Φ⁻¹(q); δ = (z_r − z_p)/(t_r − t_p), γ = z_p − δ t_p | closed form (Knoebel–Burkhart approach) |
| `johnsonsb.pct` | same formula, user-defined 0 < p < r < 1 (default 0.25, 0.75; tool convention) | closed form (general percentile method; a method separate from KB) |
| `johnsonsb.mom` | E[D] = m, Var[D] = v; with δ fixed, E[Y] decreases with γ (Brent for γ), along this curve Var[Y] decreases with δ (Brent for ln δ); δ ∈ [10⁻³, 10⁴]; v n divisor (default) or n − 1 | nested Brent, tolerance 10⁻¹⁴; one unit ≈ 20 ms |

Sample percentiles type 7. Admissibility and special cases: 0 < μ_y < 1 (`sb.mean`), 0 < v_y < μ_y(1 − μ_y) (`sb.var`), zero variance in the transformed values (`sb.degenerate`), equal percentiles (`q.tied`), δ outside the search interval (`sb.boundary`, "at interval boundary"), Brent fails (`sb.noconverge`). In none of them are parameters given. The monotonicity assumed by the moment solver (Var[Y] decreasing with δ at fixed E[Y]) is checked in the test for three μ_y on a grid δ = e⁻⁶ … e⁹.

**Validation:** `tests/ref/make_dist_p08.py` (SciPy 1.18.1, mpmath 1.3.0): functions for six parameter sets, at points approaching the bounds to 10⁻⁹λ (on the U side SciPy is called with the reflected distribution, `johnsonsb(−γ, δ, 0, λ)` at U − x; otherwise SciPy loses digits in the difference 1 − (x − ξ)/λ); moments mpmath; CML by Nelder–Mead on (γ, ln δ) and `johnsonsb.fit` (SciPy minimiser ~10⁻⁵); percentiles by joint `least_squares` of `ppf(p) = Q_p`, `ppf(r) = Q_r`; moments by `least_squares` with the `scipy.integrate.quad` moments, the solution additionally checked with mpmath. Examples: `tests/weibull/fixture.json` (with bounds given explicitly in the test), three SB simulations with `default_rng(20261114)` (asymmetric, symmetric, left-skewed) and a sample whose bounds are 0.01 cm outside the data (not real measurements). `tests/dist/test_p08.mjs` 894/894: specification §8.1 SB row (ξ = 5, λ = 40, γ = 0.6, δ = 1.3); CML 2·10⁻⁶ against Nelder–Mead and the identity δ = 1/s_t (n divisor); KB and user pair 10⁻¹⁰ and equality of the fitted Q(p), Q(r) with the sample percentiles; moments 10⁻⁹ and equality of the fitted mean/variance with the targets; recovery of known (γ, δ) from their moments (8 cases); all special cases. `tests/dist/e2e.mjs` 68/68.

### P10: exponential and log-logistic (Fisk) (10 Nov 2026)
Specification §3.9. Sources: [R15] (NIST e-Handbook §1.3.6.6.7, exponential, location μ and scale β = 1/r; read on 22 Nov 2026) **[B]** and [R16] (SciPy `fisk` documentation, SciPy 1.18.0 manual; read on 22 Nov 2026) **[B]**: f(x, c) = c x^(c−1)/(1 + x^c)², with SciPy's scale = b the CDF 1/(1 + (d/b)^(−c)) here; the equations were also verified independently with SciPy and mpmath.

**Exponential** (`dist/families/exponential.js`): a location (cm), r > 0 **rate** (1/cm); F(d) = 1 − exp[−r(d − a)], d ≥ a. **Scale 1/r** (cm) is written separately so that it is not confused with the rate (`diagnostics.scale`, on screen "scale 1/r = … cm"); SciPy mapping `expon(loc = a, scale = 1/r)`. ln F, for u = r(d − a), at u < ln 2 ln(−expm1(−u)), above that log1p(−e^(−u)). Offered as a simple comparison model for reverse-J shaped stands.

| Method | Equation | Note |
|---|---|---|
| `exponential.ml` | r = 1/(m − a) | The method of moments (E[D] = a + 1/r) gives the same equation; as for the normal, a single method is registered, on screen "Maximum likelihood (= moments)". a is not estimated: a = 0 always, and if the user types one also with fixed a (the same fixed-location card and the same rule as Weibull: a ≥ 0, smaller than the smallest diameter). AIC/BIC k = 1. |

A linear fit to the logarithm of class frequencies is not offered (it is a regression on grouped counts, not the maximum likelihood estimate from raw diameters; specification §3.9).

**Log-logistic / Fisk** (`dist/families/loglogistic.js`): b > 0 scale and median (cm), c > 0 shape; no location. z = c(ln d − ln b), F = logistic(z), f = (c/d)·g(z), ln g(z) = −|z| − 2·log1p(e^(−|z|)); ln F = −log1p(e^(−z)) without overflow in both tails; Q_p = b·exp[logit(p)/c]. SciPy `fisk(c, scale = b)`.
- **Moments:** E[D] = b·θ/sin θ exists only for c > 1, Var[D] = b²[2θ/sin 2θ − (θ/sin θ)²] only for c > 2 (θ = π/c). Otherwise `mean()`/`variance()` throw the error `moment.undefined` (they do not return a number, NaN or infinity). In every result `diagnostics.moments` = { whether the mean exists, whether the variance exists, their values if they exist, null otherwise }; on screen, for c ≤ 1 the note "mean and variance do not exist", for 1 < c ≤ 2 "the mean exists but the variance does not; Dq cannot be computed". For θ < 0.01 (c > 314) the variance bracket by the series θ²/3 + 11θ⁴/45 + 38θ⁶/315 (no cancellation loss; coefficients verified with mpmath).

| Method | Equation | Solver |
|---|---|---|
| `loglogistic.ml` | y = ln d, z = c·y − β (β = c ln b): ℓ = n ln c + Σ ln g(z_i) − Σ y_i; jointly concave in (β, c) (ln g concave, z linear, ln c concave), single maximum | Newton, exact Hessian, step halving; with y centred on its mean (conditioning); starts from the log-moment estimate; score per tree < 10⁻¹⁴; c > 10⁶ → boundary status (`ll.boundary`), if it does not converge `ll.noconverge` |
| `loglogistic.pct` | c = [logit r − logit p]/ln(Q_r/Q_p), b = Q_p/[p/(1 − p)]^(1/c); sample percentiles type 7; default 0.25/0.75 (quartiles; **tool convention**, not a best pair from a source) | closed form |
| `loglogistic.logmom` | **log-moments**: b = exp[mean(ln d)], c = π/√[3·var_n(ln d)] (n divisor); ln D is logistically distributed, the mean and variance of the logarithms are matched | closed form |

**Not offered:** the ordinary method of moments with the mean and variance of the diameters (defined only for c > 2; specification §3.9). Log-moments are shown under a name distinct from it ("not ordinary moments"). **Recovery:** the two-percentile formula (`llPercentileParams`) returns the parameters from theoretical percentiles (tested); it was not added at this stage to the "recovery from summaries" workflow that works with summary values, because that workflow takes mean + variance/Dq input (percentile input with Weibull recovery, in P06). For the exponential, "recovery from the mean" was likewise not added for the same reason.

**Validation:** `tests/ref/make_dist_p10.py` (SciPy 1.18.1, NumPy 2.5.3, mpmath 1.4.1): exponential functions with `expon` in both tails (down to 10⁻²⁰⁰); log-logistic functions with mpmath at 40 digits (SciPy `fisk` loses digits at the 10⁻¹⁴ level in the upper tail because it computes sf from 1 − cdf; SciPy was additionally used for comparison in the body of the distribution); exponential maximum likelihood `expon.fit(floc = a)` and `minimize_scalar` on ln r; log-logistic maximum likelihood by Nelder–Mead on (ln b, ln c) and `fisk.fit(floc = 0)`; two percentiles by the joint `least_squares` solution of the equations `fisk.ppf(p) = Q_p`, `ppf(r) = Q_r` (closed form not used); log-moments with `logistic.stats` and `least_squares`; moments and their existence by numerical integration of ∫dᵏf(d)dd with mpmath; recovery from theoretical percentiles with five parameter sets × four percentile pairs. Examples: `tests/weibull/fixture.json` and four simulations with `default_rng(20261113)` (c = 3.5; c = 1.6, mean exists, variance does not; c = 0.9, no mean; reverse J above a = 7; not real measurements). `tests/dist/test_p10.mjs` 1025/1025: §8.1 exponential and log-logistic rows, functions (relative 10⁻¹²–10⁻¹³), maximum likelihood (2·10⁻⁷ against Nelder–Mead, ln L not smaller than either SciPy optimum; Newton < 50 steps), percentiles (10⁻¹⁰), log-moments (10⁻¹¹), moment existence c = 0.9, 1, 1.6, 2, 3, 3.5, 12, 500, statuses (n < 2, equal diameters, zero diameter, a not smaller than the smallest diameter, equal percentiles, p ≥ r). `tests/dist/e2e.mjs` 76/76 (two families and four methods in the selector, a = 0 and user a for the exponential, r (1/cm) column and the scale note, log-logistic values identical to the engine, AIC only for maximum likelihood).

### P06: Weibull recovery, Poudel and Cao (2013) M2–M5 (10 Nov 2026)
Specification §3.6. Source: Poudel, K.P., Cao, Q.V. 2013. Evaluation of methods to predict Weibull parameters for characterizing diameter distributions. *Forest Science* 59(2): 243–252 ([R1]) **[B]** (read while the specification was prepared; Table 2 and pp. 245–247). The b/c naming mix-up in the footnote of Table 2 was used in corrected form: b scale, c shape.

**Methods** (`dist/recover/weibull.js`; kind `recover`, separate ids; G_r = Γ(1 + r/c), A_p = −ln(1 − p)):

| Method | Targets | Equation | Solver |
|---|---|---|---|
| `weibull.m2` | mean m, variance v | b = (m − a)/G₁; b²(G₂ − G₁²) = v, for c | grid + Brent |
| `weibull.m3` | Dq = q, variance v | b = B_q(c) = [−aG₁ + √(a²G₁² + G₂(q² − a²))]/G₂ (positive root); b²(G₂ − G₁²) = v, for c | grid + Brent |
| `weibull.m4` | Q31, Q63 | c = ln(A.63/A.31)/ln[(Q63 − a)/(Q31 − a)], b = (Q31 − a)/A.31^(1/c) | closed form |
| `weibull.m5` | Q50, Q95 | same, p = 0.50, r = 0.95 | closed form |

- **The location policy is chosen separately** (it is not part of the method; `fixed.a.policy`): `half-dmin` a = 0.5 × Dmin (the source's M2–M9 policy; `origin: 'rule'`, Dmin is written to the record) or `user` (the a given by the user; 0 = two-parameter form; not labelled "the source's policy"). **Dmin** is the stand's smallest-diameter target (D0 in the source; on the platform D0 is the dominant diameter); it is not the distribution's theoretical Q(0) = a.
- **Origin of the targets** in every result: `targets.origin` = `measured` (measured on the plot) or `predicted` (the output of a stand model); `targets.from` = `typed` or `raw` (computed from the loaded diameters). From raw data: mean, variance **with n divisor**, Dq = √(m² + v), Dmin = smallest diameter, percentiles Hyndman and Fan type 7 (`targets.quantile`). The same methods also work with raw data input (`runMethod(id, { type: 'raw', d })`); this is not a stand regression (that is P16).
- **Variance:** moment variance v (n divisor); if s² with the n − 1 divisor is given, v = s²(n − 1)/n, n required.
- **Root search:** for M2/M3 c ∈ [0.05, 200] (tool convention; covers the interval [0.15, 50] of specification §8.2), sign changes on a 600-point geometric grid, each by Brent (x tol 10⁻¹⁵); all roots are kept, repeated ones are merged. Single root → `ok`; no root → `invalid` (`wsum.noroot`, interval in the record); more than one → new status **`multiple`** (candidates `diagnostics.candidates`, no parameters given; prepared for M8/M9 in P07). The equation is written in relative form (b²(G₂ − G₁²)/v − 1). The grid can miss double roots narrower than the grid spacing (known limitation; in M2 the equation has a single root, in M3 SciPy also found a single root with a 4000-point scan in all examples in the test).
- **Checks:** m > a, q > a, Q_p > a, Q_r > Q_p, a ≥ 0, Dmin > 0, n ≥ 2 integer; each solution is checked by back-calculating the targets (relative residual ≤ 10⁻⁹, otherwise `noconverge`). The back-calculated mean, variance, Dq and Q31/Q63/Q50/Q95 are in `diagnostics`.
- **Relation to direct estimation:** with the same a, M2 = `weibull.mom` (n divisor), M4 = `weibull.pct` (p = 0.31, r = 0.63); these equalities were verified on raw data in the test. Recovery remains a separate kind (the targets may have been predicted).
- **Interface:** a separate card in the "Recovery from summaries" tab: method selection, only the fields required by the selected methods, location policy (Dmin or a), origin of the targets, "compute targets from loaded raw data" (unit selection; if a target is changed by hand the record becomes "entered by hand"), in the result table target → back-calculated, number of roots and interval, density plot, JSON.
- **Version** 2.0.0-beta.3 (the method set changed).

**Validation:** `tests/ref/make_dist_p06.py` (SciPy 1.18.1, NumPy 2.5.3) does not use the G-function reduction, the B_q formula or the closed forms of the JS: targets from the `weibull_min` moments and `ppf`; solutions by the joint `least_squares` solution of the two targets on (ln b, ln c); the number of M2/M3 roots by a 4000-point scan of the equation written from `weibull_min.stats`; raw data summaries numpy (`var` ddof 0, `quantile(method='linear')`). `tests/dist/test_p06.mjs` 491/491: from the §8.2 targets, a = 5, b = 20, c = 2.5 with the four methods, both with a = 5 and with Dmin = 10 (a = 0.5 × Dmin) (10⁻¹²), seven known Weibulls (c = 0.8 … 40), targets and results from raw data in four samples (two location policies), the equalities M2 = moment fit and M4 = percentile fit, statuses (missing target, only the required fields, no Dmin, a negative or larger than the targets, unordered/equal percentiles, no root, no n for s², n = 1), multiple-root scan. `tests/dist/e2e.mjs` 88/88. The "no Weibull recovery yet" checks in the P01, P02, P05 tests were made independent of the registry.

### Export: Excel and CSV (10 Nov 2026, user request; 2.0.0-beta.4)
At the user's priority the export part of P15 was brought forward (class tables remain in P12, the curve explorer in P15). Tables are produced DOM-free in `dist/report.js`; the interface only supplies the buttons and file names. The numbers are the engine's values, written **at full precision and as numbers** (number cells in Excel; in CSV the shortest exact decimal representation, decimal point, comma field separator, UTF-8 BOM; `core/export.js`). Labels are in the interface language; next to them are language-independent codes (`method_id`, `status`, `parameter`), so TR and EN files carry the same values. In results whose status is not "ok", the parameter, ln L and goodness-of-fit cells stay empty, and the reason is written in the "Reason / note" column.

- **Fitting to measured diameters** (all plot × period units): Excel report `Info` (tool version, date, data, columns, unit, unit of analysis, decimal separator, Weibull locations, bounds fixed by the user, selected methods and their options, numbers of read/unreadable diameters and source rows not entering the analysis, definition notes), `Fits` (one row per unit × result: distribution, method, estimation principle, status and its code, reason, up to four parameter name–value pairs, fixed parameters and their origins, ln L, k, AIC, BIC, KS D, AD A², P(D < 0) for the normal, solver, iterations, settings e.g. variance divisor and percentile pair), `Parameters` (long format: one row per parameter, unit, role, "estimated" or fixed origin; log scale for the lognormal), `Plots` (n, distinct values, min, max, mean, Dq, variance with n and n − 1 divisor, status). CSV: `Fits` and `Parameters` as separate files. JSON result records (contract `dist-result/1`) continue unchanged.
- **Recovery from summaries** and **Weibull recovery (M2–M5)**: Excel (`Info`: inputs, location policy, origin of the targets; `Recovery`) and CSV. Per row: distribution, method, status, reason, parameters, fixed location and its policy (Dmin included), each numerical target and its back-calculated value (columns from the union of the results), all candidate solutions in the "multiple solutions" case, solver, settings (origin of the targets, source, percentile definition).
- **Validation:** `tests/dist/test_export.mjs` 188/188: every number written is identical to the engine's value (parameters, ln L, KS, AD, AIC/BIC only for methods that maximise the likelihood, P(D < 0) only for the normal), empty cell and reason for failed fits, identity of the numbers in tables produced with Turkish labels and without labels, the CSV giving the same numbers when read back, long-format parameter table, unit table, recovery tables (including the invalid and "multiple solutions" cases). `tests/dist/e2e.mjs` 101/101: the downloaded Excel file opened with SheetJS and its sheet names, the exact values of numeric cells, failed rows, the Info sheet; BOM and full precision in CSV; Excel/CSV of the two recovery workflows.

### P07: Weibull hybrid recovery, Poudel and Cao (2013) M6–M10 (10 Nov 2026; 2.0.0-beta.5)
Source: Poudel and Cao (2013) Table 2 **[B]** (specification §3.6). The infrastructure of P06 (targets, location policy, root scan, `multiple` status, back-calculation) is used; `dist/recover/weibull.js`. G_r(c) = Γ(1 + r/c), A_p = −ln(1 − p), C_{p,r}(a) = ln(A_r/A_p)/ln[(Q_r − a)/(Q_p − a)], B_q(c) = [−aG1 + √(a²G1² + G2(q² − a²))]/G2.

| Method | Inputs | Location | Shape and scale | Matched by construction |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `weibull.m6` | Dq, Q25, Q95 | 0.5 × Dmin or the user's a | c = C_.25,.95(a), b = B_q(c) | Dq, percentile ratio |
| `weibull.m7` | Dq, Q31, Q63 | same | c = C_.31,.63(a), b = B_q(c) | Dq, percentile ratio |
| `weibull.m8` | mean, Q95 | same | b = (Q95 − a)/A.95^(1/c); a + bG1 = mean, for c | mean, Q95 |
| `weibull.m9` | Dq, Q95 | same | same b; a² + 2abG1 + b²G2 = Dq², for c | Dq, Q95 |
| `weibull.m10` | Dq, Q25, Q50, Q95, Dmin, n | **its own rule**: a = (n^(1/3)·Dmin − Q50)/(n^(1/3) − 1) | c = C_.25,.95(a), b = B_q(c) | Dq, percentile ratio |

- **Hybrid:** shape and scale come from different targets; an exact match of all targets is not expected. Only the targets matched by construction (`exact`) are checked by back-calculation (relative 10⁻⁹); the other targets and their back-calculated values are shown and marked on screen with "*", with the note "not matched by construction".
- **M8/M9 multiple roots:** all roots are searched in the interval c ∈ [0.05, 200] with a 600-point geometric grid + Brent, repeated ones are merged. Single root → "ok"; more than one → `multiple` (no parameters given, candidates listed with their back-calculated values); no root → "not computed" (`wsum.noroot`). The interval, the grid and the selection rule ("no selection; no hidden c > 1 rule") are in the result record (`convergence.interval`, `grid`, `selection`). **Finding:** in all four samples of `tests/weibull/fixture.json` and in the specification §8.2 example, M8 and M9 have two positive roots (the small root c ≈ 0.2–0.4); on measured data these methods mostly give `multiple`.
- **M10:** n is the number of trees measured on the plot (integer, ≥ 2; the per-hectare number is not accepted), Dmin the smallest diameter; both are required. The location option is not used; the record contains `fixed.a.policy = 'm10'`, Dmin, n and Q50. If the formula gives a negative a (`m10.aneg`) or if a ≥ Q25, no result is given, a is not pulled to zero; if a ≥ Dmin, a warning (`m10.aabove`).
- Hybrid methods are not selected by default in the interface (they require extra inputs, M8/M9 mostly give multiple solutions). Filling targets from raw data now also computes Q25 (type 7).
- **Validation:** `tests/ref/make_dist_p07.py` (SciPy 1.18.1) does not use the closed forms of the JS: shape as the `brentq` root of the equation setting the ratio `weibull_min(c).ppf(r)/ppf(p)` equal to the target ratio; scale as the root of `weibull_min(c, a, b).moment(2) = Dq²`; M8/M9 roots by a 4000-point grid + `brentq`. `tests/dist/test_p07.mjs` 235/235: specification §8.2 (M6, M7, M10 → b = 20, c = 2.5; M10 a = 5 [n = 125, Dmin = 8.4545396024095]; M8 roots 0.191476498065 and 2.5, M9 roots 0.398620001976 and 2.5, both `multiple`), 10⁻¹⁰ against SciPy on the fixture samples (parameters, candidate roots, back-calculated values), the targets matched by construction and that the other targets are not forced to match, no root, negative M10 location, n = 1, non-integer n, missing Dmin/n, a ≥ Q25. `tests/dist/e2e.mjs` 107/107.

### P09: Johnson SB recovery, Parresol (2003), three parameters with ξ fixed (10 Nov 2026; 2.0.0-beta.6)
Source: Parresol (2003) Equations 9–16 **[B]** (specification §3.8). `dist/recover/parresol.js`, method `johnsonsb.parresol` (kind `recover`, data type `summary`; a separate card in the recovery-from-summaries workflow).

- **Definition:** "three-parameter recovery with ξ fixed". ξ (lower bound) is a fixed value given by the user (`fixed.xi`, origin `user`); λ, γ, δ are recovered. Parresol's species-specific regression coefficients, starting equations and the lower-bound estimate from the three smallest diameters **are not used** and are not embedded. Since the mean, variance and second raw moment are not three independent pieces of information, it is not claimed that the fourth parameter (ξ) is solved for.
- **Inputs:** ξ, mean diameter m, median M; Dq either directly or from N (trees/ha) and G (m²/ha): Dq² = G/(K·N), K = π/40000. If both are entered the relative difference must be at most 10⁻³ (tool convention; if exceeded `prs.dqmismatch`, both values are written), and the entered Dq is used in the computation. N is used here only for Dq, it is not the sample size. Filling from raw data computes the mean, median (type 7) and Dq; ξ is entered by the user.
- **Equations:** γ = δ ln[(ξ + λ − M)/(M − ξ)] (the fitted median equals M exactly; λ > M − ξ is required). Z ~ N(0, 1), Y = logistic((Z − γ)/δ), u_r = E[Y^r] (composite Gauss–Legendre as in P08): ξ + λu₁ = m and ξ² + 2ξλu₁ + λ²u₂ = Dq².
- **Solver:** bivariate Newton (finite-difference Jacobian, step halving) on s, t, λ = (M − ξ) + eˢ, δ = eᵗ (positivity by transformation); scaled residuals (m₁/m − 1, m₂/Dq² − 1), convergence 10⁻¹²; **12 starts** ((λ − (M − ξ))/(M − ξ) ∈ {0.25, 1, 4, 16} × δ ∈ {0.3, 1, 3}). Distinct solutions are collected (further apart than relative 10⁻⁷): one → "ok", more than one → `multiple` (candidates, no parameters), none → "did not converge" (`prs.noconverge`). The accepted solution is rechecked in the original units (m, M, Dq relative 10⁻⁹). The condition number of the Jacobian at the solution is written to the record; if it exceeds 10⁸, a weak identifiability warning (`prs.ident`; tool convention).
- **Necessary conditions** (if not satisfied, "not computed"): ξ ≥ 0, m > ξ, M > ξ, Dq > m, N > 0, G > 0. Satisfying them does not guarantee a single or an admissible solution.
- **Finding:** even flawless inputs generated from the known SB parameters (γ = −1.5, δ = 0.9; ξ = 2, λ = 48) have two solutions of the Parresol equations: (λ, δ) = (48, 0.9) and (40.3746, 0.1393); the second was verified with mpmath at 40 digits (residual 10⁻³⁸). In this case the tool gives `multiple` and shows both candidates. The first version of the reference missed this root because it did not subdivide the integral enough at small δ; the corrected reference (γ ± kδ breakpoints, starts beginning at δ 0.08) finds the same two roots as the JS.
- **Validation:** `tests/ref/make_dist_p09.py` (SciPy 1.18.1, mpmath 1.3.0): u₁, u₂ with `scipy.integrate.quad` (γ ± kδ breakpoints), `least_squares` from 7 × 8 starts; each solution is re-evaluated with mpmath at 40 digits. Cases: specification §8.3, five known SB parameter sets (with N, G), summaries of `tests/weibull/fixture.json` with ξ = 0 and ξ = 0.5·min (one without a solution). `tests/dist/test_p09.mjs` 122/122: from §8.3 λ = 40, γ = 0.6, δ = 1.3 (10⁻¹²) and back-verification of m, median, Dq; Dq from N and G; in all cases the number of solutions and each solution matching SciPy (10⁻⁸); the generating parameters being among the solution(s); all input checks; the case without a solution although the conditions are satisfied. `tests/dist/e2e.mjs` 114/114.

### P11: measurement threshold and truncated distributions (11 Nov 2026; 2.0.0-beta.7)
Specification §2.2–2.3. The threshold is a property of the **observation** (only trees with L ≤ d ≤ U were measured); it is kept separate from the support bound of the distribution (Weibull/exponential a, beta L/U, Johnson SB ξ/λ) and from the sample minimum/maximum. If there is no U, ∞.

- **Conditional distribution** (`dist/truncate.js`): f_T(d) = f(d)/[F(U) − F(L)], F_T(d) = [F(d) − F(L)]/[F(U) − F(L)]. The mass ln[F(U) − F(L)] is computed from the correct side: if F(L) < ½, ln F(U) + ln(1 − e^{ln F(L) − ln F(U)}), otherwise from the survival function ln S(L) + ln(1 − e^{ln S(U) − ln S(L)}); ln(1 − eˣ) in the form of Mächler (2012) **[D]**. In the extreme tails the mass is within 10⁻¹¹ of mpmath (50 digits) (e.g. for the normal P(10 ≤ Z ≤ 12) ≈ 7.6·10⁻²⁴). Conditional quantile: on the lower side F⁻¹(F(L) + q·mass), on the upper side F⁻¹(1 − [S(U) + (1 − q)·mass]).
- **Conditional maximum likelihood** (`dist/fit/truncated.js`), `<family>.tml` for each family: Σ ln f(d; θ) − n ln[F(U; θ) − F(L; θ)] is maximised. The family's fixed parameters are as in its untruncated methods (Weibull/exponential a = 0 or the user's a, beta L/U, Johnson SB ξ/λ from the user). Parameters bounded below by 0 on the log scale, the others (normal/lognormal μ, Johnson SB γ) as they are. Start: the untruncated maximum likelihood estimate on the same data (only a start, not a result). Solver: Nelder–Mead (`core/optim.js`; reflection 1, expansion 2, contraction ½, shrinkage ½) and restarting until there is no further improvement. AIC/BIC with k = number of free parameters; ln L is the conditional likelihood.
- **Result record:** the parameters of the underlying distribution (`params`), and the threshold and information on the observed conditional distribution (`truncation`: lower, upper, ln mass, mass = P(L ≤ D ≤ U), observed n, n/mass = estimated total including those outside the threshold) are in separate fields. KS D and AD A² are computed against the conditional distribution; the plots draw the conditional density and CDF.
- **The scope of N** is chosen explicitly: "trees above the threshold (measured n)" or "the whole population (estimated with n/mass)"; in the record `settings.nScope`, in Excel/CSV the column "N (selected scope)".
- **Disabled:** when a threshold is declared, the closed-form methods valid for untruncated data (ML, moments, percentiles, including the Johnson SB/beta methods) do not run and are shown greyed out in the settings with the reason; in the recovery-from-summaries cards "fill from raw data" is disabled (the mean and percentiles of a thresholded sample are not summaries of the whole distribution). Without a threshold the `.tml` methods are not listed (they would be identical to untruncated ML).
- **Special cases:** no threshold entered (`trunc.none`), invalid/reversed threshold, observation outside the threshold (`trunc.outside`, count and example; observations are not changed), no starting estimate, maximisation failed (`trunc.noconverge`), parameter escaped to a boundary (`trunc.boundary`, |ln θ| > 60; tool convention).
- **Validation:** `tests/ref/make_dist_p11.py` (SciPy 1.18.1, mpmath): for each of the eight families two simulated samples (d ≥ 8 cm and 10 ≤ d ≤ 40 cm; by the rejection method, rounded to 0.1 cm; `default_rng(20261111)`; not real measurements), the conditional likelihood maximised with SciPy's own cdf/sf/logpdf by Nelder–Mead + Powell; extreme-tail masses mpmath. `tests/dist/test_p11.mjs` 419/419: integral of the conditional density over the observation interval 1 (2·10⁻⁹), F_T(Q_T(q)) = q (10⁻⁸), F_T + S_T = 1, 0/1 outside the interval; conditional ln L and mass at the SciPy estimate 10⁻¹⁰; conditional ML parameters 5·10⁻⁶ against SciPy and ln L not smaller than SciPy's; untruncated ML giving a different result that is further from the true value (Weibull); all special cases. `tests/dist/e2e.mjs` 126/126 (methods being disabled, KS against the conditional distribution, Excel columns, filling being disabled in recovery).

### P12: diameter classes and grouped data (11 Nov 2026; 2.0.0-beta.8)
Specification §2.3. `dist/classes.js`, `dist/fit/grouped.js`, `dist/data.js` (`extractClassUnits`).

- **Class:** interval [lower, upper) (cm); widths may differ; only the last class may be open-ended (upper = ∞). Overlapping classes are rejected; gaps between classes are not classes and are shown in a separate row. Probability p_j = F(upper) − F(lower), on the log scale from the correct side (`truncate.js` `logMass`); **there is no correction constant (e.g. "+1") or midpoint correction**: when the class width changes, only the CDF difference changes.
- **Class table** (the "Classes" tab for raw data; class definition automatic [same as the histogram], start and width, or a list of bounds): for each fit the observed count, probability, expected = n·p_j, optional N/ha (multiplier 10000/plot size in m²; n does not change), lower tail P(D < first lower), upper tail P(D ≥ last upper; absent for an open-ended last class), gap probability (from its own CDF differences), totals and Σ|observed − expected| (per hectare: the EI of Poudel and Cao). No rescaling is done: when the support is fully covered, the total probability is 1 and the expected total is n (N in N/ha). The histogram, expected counts, N/ha and tail probability are produced from the same class edges. For fits with a measurement threshold, the probabilities are from the conditional distribution.
- **Grouped data input** ("Data type: classified frequencies"; each row one class: lower bound, upper bound [empty = open-ended], frequency; plot/period columns optional; in the pool identical classes are summed): `<family>.gml` for each family, Σ n_j ln p_j is maximised (Nelder–Mead, positive parameters on the log scale; the family's fixed parameters as in the raw data methods). Start: raw data ML at the class midpoints, **only a start**; treating the midpoints as raw diameters is not offered as an estimate (the test checks that it gives a different result).
  - **Frequency type:** "number of trees measured" (integer required): ln L = Σ n_j ln p_j (the multinomial constant ln[n!/Πn_j!] does not depend on the parameters and is dropped; it is the same in all models on the same classes), AIC/BIC with n = Σ n_j. "Frequency expanded to per hectare": the same function with weights gives the parameters (independent of the scale of the weights), but since the actual number of trees is unknown, ln L, AIC and BIC are **not given**; KS/AD are never given for classed data.
  - **Range outside the classes:** "measured, no trees" (p_j unconditional) or "not recorded" (p_j / Σ p_k, conditional on the listed classes). Rows with zero frequency are measured, empty classes.
  - Grouped data have no measurement-threshold card (the same information is given with the "not recorded" option); the histogram bars are the user's classes (an open-ended last class is not drawn), the cumulative plot shows the proportions at the class bounds.
- **Excel:** "Classes" sheet (unit × fit × class: bounds, observed, probability, expected, N/ha; tail and gap rows).
- **Validation:** `tests/ref/make_dist_p12.py` (SciPy 1.18.1): class probabilities as `cdf` differences, grouped likelihood with Nelder–Mead + Powell for eight families × two outside-the-classes options + per-hectare expanded frequency (×25); classes 4, 8, 12, 16, 20, 26, 32, 40, ∞ (unequal, open-ended last); simulations of 500 trees (`default_rng(20261112)`; not real measurements). `tests/dist/test_p12.mjs` 303/303: hand-computed Weibull(0, 20, 2.5) probabilities and Σ n_j ln p_j (10⁻¹⁴), CDF difference at different widths and the sum of merged classes, SciPy probabilities (10⁻¹¹), total probability 1 and the expected N/ha total, gap and tail probabilities, grouped ML 5·10⁻⁶ against SciPy, identical parameters and absence of ln L for per-hectare frequency, the difference of the midpoint method, all checks. `tests/dist/e2e.mjs` 137/137.

### P13: model comparison and diagnostic plots (11 Nov 2026; 2.0.0-beta.9)
Specification §2.4. `dist/compare.js`, `dist/gof.js`.

- **Goodness-of-fit measures:** KS D = max(D⁺, D⁻), D⁺ = max[j/n − F(d₍ⱼ₎)], D⁻ = max[F(d₍ⱼ₎) − (j − 1)/n] (two-sided); AD A²; **Cramér–von Mises** W² = 1/(12n) + Σ[F(d₍ᵢ₎) − (2i − 1)/(2n)]². For fits with a measurement threshold, all three are computed with the conditional distribution. For classed data KS/AD/CvM are not given. **No p-value is given** (the parameters were estimated from the same data; bootstrap P14).
- **Class errors:** with the shared class edges of the "Classes" tab, excluding the tail rows, in the same way for all models: Σ|O − E| (with raw counts, unit trees) and, if the plot size was entered, **EI** (Poudel and Cao) = Σ|N_observed − N_expected|, unit trees/ha (= Σ|O − E| × 10000/area). For classed data with per-hectare frequencies, Σ|O − E| is already in trees/ha and is shown as EI. The two are presented with separate names and units.
- **Comparison table** ("Comparison" tab, Excel "Comparison" sheet): results are grouped by **observation process** (data type, measurement thresholds, outside-the-classes option, frequency type); different processes are not mixed in the same ranking. In each group:
  - maximum likelihood fits (maximising the likelihood, ln L on the diameter scale and n known): n, k (free parameters; bounds and location fixed by the user do not count in k), ln L, AIC, ΔAIC, BIC, ΔBIC (relative to the smallest in the group), AICc = AIC + 2k(k + 1)/(n − k − 1) (only for n > k + 1; that it was derived for regular models is stated on screen), KS, AD, CvM, class errors, solver;
  - descriptive comparison (moment, percentile, log-moment; no AIC/BIC, ln L as a "log score");
  - those that gave no result, with their statuses and reasons (non-convergence, boundary solution, multiple solutions, invalid).
  **There is no composite "best model" score or rank field**; the table is sorted by the single criterion chosen by the user, and which criterion it is ("smaller/larger is better") is written above the table (default AIC).
- **Plots:** histogram on two scales, not mixed: density (1/cm; count/(n × width), curves are probability densities) or count (trees; observed class counts and expected n·[F(upper) − F(lower)] points). Both use the edges of the "Classes" tab. For raw data, **Q–Q** (x: theoretical quantile Q((i − 0.5)/n), y: observed diameter; both in cm) and **P–P** (x: F(d₍ᵢ₎), y: (i − 0.5)/n) plots, for a selected fit, with the 1:1 line.
- **Lognormal Jacobian:** ln L(lognormal; d) = ln L(normal; ln d) − Σ ln d is checked in the test.
- **Validation:** `tests/ref/make_dist_p13.py` (SciPy 1.18.1): `kstest`, `cramervonmises`, AD (logcdf/logsf), ln L and Q–Q/P–P points for four families with fixed parameters on the `tests/weibull/fixture.json` samples, also a conditional (truncated) Weibull. `tests/dist/test_p13.mjs` 764/764: KS 10⁻¹², CvM and AD 10⁻¹⁰, ln L 10⁻¹¹, a hand KS example (determined by D⁻), AICc and n ≤ k + 1, EI = L1 × 10000/area (trees/ha) by hand, grouping (thresholded fit a separate group; ML and descriptive separate; failures separate), ΔAIC, sort directions, absence of a composite score and of a p-value field. `tests/dist/e2e.mjs` 146/146.

### P14: bootstrap and uncertainty (11 Nov 2026; 2.0.0-beta.10)
Specification §2.4; general source [R9] (SciPy `goodness_of_fit` documentation, SciPy 1.18.0 manual; read on 22 Nov 2026) **[B]**: the Monte Carlo (parametric bootstrap) test refits all unknown parameters to every sample of the same size and gives p = (b + 1)/(m + 1) (Phipson and Smyth 2010), as here. `dist/bootstrap.js` (DOM-free), `dist/boot-worker.js` (Web Worker), the "Uncertainty" tab in the interface.

- **Two separate operations:** (1) goodness-of-fit test p-values (parametric bootstrap only), (2) parameter and percentile intervals (parametric or nonparametric). Separate buttons, separate result records.
- **Parametric bootstrap process:** in each replicate, data of the same size are generated from the fitted **observation model** (for a fit with a measurement threshold from the conditional distribution; for classed data (frequency type "number of trees", outside-the-classes "not recorded") class counts by draws conditional on the listed classes (a draw falling into a gap is redrawn)), the same **measurement rounding** is applied (floor(x/step + 0.5)·step; step from the data: the largest of 1, 0.1, 0.01, 0.001 cm of which all values are multiples; or the user's value; or none), the same method is rerun **with the same options** (a, beta L/U, Johnson SB ξ/λ fixed by the user, and the thresholds stay fixed), and the statistics are recomputed against the refitted model (for raw data KS, AD, CvM; for classed data Pearson X² = Σ(O − E)²/E). Random numbers mulberry32(seed) (`core/stats.js`); u = (k + 0.5)/2³² (never 0 or 1); draws by the inverse CDF.
- **p-value:** p = (1 + #{T_b ≥ T₀})/(B_ok + 1); Monte Carlo SE = √[p(1 − p)/B_ok].
- **Intervals:** for the estimated (free) parameters and the diameter percentiles Q25, Q50, Q75, Q95 of the fitted distribution, the 2.5% and 97.5% percentiles of the replicates (type 7) and the bootstrap SE (n − 1 divisor). The nonparametric scheme resamples trees **within the unit** with replacement; for multi-plot stand models, resampling at plot level is a separate scheme and is not done here. No Wald interval is given.
- **Record:** seed, requested B, completed replicates, failed replicates by status (did not converge, at boundary, multiple solutions, not computed, error), failure rate, rounding step used. If the failure rate exceeds 5%, a warning (tool convention); if fewer than 20 replicates are completed, no p-value/interval is given (tool convention). A result with multiple solutions is not resolved by a rule: that replicate is always a "multiple solutions" failure (the same rule in all replicates).
- **Not supported** (an explanation instead of a number): failed fit; recovery-from-summaries methods (there is no data-generating process in the summary workflow); classed data with per-hectare frequencies (the actual number of trees is unknown); the outside-the-classes option "measured, no trees"; the nonparametric scheme for classed data.
- **Execution:** in a Web Worker (module worker); progress is reported every 5 replicates; "Cancel" terminates the worker and no partial result is shown.
- **Finding:** for distributions with high density near zero, such as the exponential (a = 0), 0.1 cm rounding turns some simulated diameters into 0.0; these replicates are not valid data (diameter > a), they are counted as "not computed" and the rate is shown with a warning (31 of 99 replicates in the fixture sample "w2").
- **Validation:** `tests/ref/make_dist_p14.py` rebuilds the algorithm from its definition with SciPy and uses **the same random stream** (mulberry32 and the same inverse-CDF draws); refitting (`norm.fit`, `weibull_min.fit(floc=0)`, exponential r = 1/mean), the statistics (`kstest`, `cramervonmises`, AD) and the intervals (`numpy.quantile` linear) are independent. `tests/dist/test_p14.mjs` 98/98: for normal, exponential and Weibull the counts of T ≥ T₀ identical to SciPy, p and Monte Carlo SE, parametric and nonparametric intervals 10⁻¹⁰; same seed → same result, another seed → another result; re-estimation in every replicate (different estimates, the method runs once per replicate); for a thresholded fit, replicates coming from the conditional model and rounded; for classed data the same classes and total; counting and warning for replicates rounded onto a fixed bound; unsupported combinations; no p with fewer than 20 replicates. `tests/dist/e2e.mjs` 154/154 (worker result = engine, same seed same table, cancel, X² for classed data, explanation for an unsupported combination).

### P15: interface, output and first-release validation (11 Nov 2026; 2.0.0)
Specification P15. Short report on scope and remaining items: `docs/DIST_P15.md`.

- **Source display** (`dist/sources.js`, DOM-free): for each registered method the sources and the section in this document (§12 P05 … P14); references, links and reading status are copied only from the DIST_SPEC.md source list and from this document ([B] read: Poudel and Cao 2013, Parresol 2003, Dubey 1967; the others [D]). In the interface, below each result set, "Methods, units and sources": method, family, estimation principle, solver, parameter units, support policy (a fixed; beta L/U and SB ξ/λ from the user; for the normal the probability of a negative diameter), observation (raw diameters without threshold, measurement threshold and conditional distribution, class frequencies, summaries) and source numbers. [R9] in the Bootstrap tab.
- **Export:** "Bootstrap" sheets in Excel (one row for each unit × fit × operation × statistic/quantity; seed, requested B, completed, failures by status, failure rate, rounding step and scheme in every row; numbers as number cells) and "Sources" sheets; "Sources" in the recovery Excel files. In the JSON records all settings (data type, frequency type, outside-the-classes, threshold), bootstrap runs and sources. Inside the SVG plots as `<metadata>`: tool version, settings and the full-precision parameters of the plotted fits. Decimal point in CSV (unchanged).
- **Status distinction:** for each unit the number of computed and not computable/unsupported results; parameters are shown only with the status "ok".
- **Fixes:** on recomputation the bootstrap results of the previous computation are deleted (formerly they could be shown as belonging to the new computation under the same method key); an explanation instead of an empty result page when there are no readable diameters or classes; accessible name for the paste box (`core/importer.js`, all tools).
- **Loading warning:** if the page is not ready within 15 seconds, or if the program file returns something other than JavaScript (e.g. the hosting service's security page), a "tool could not be loaded" warning and a reload button.
- **Validation:** `tests/dist/test_p15.mjs` 144/144. This test checks:
  - every method has a source and a document section;
  - every reference, link and [B]/[D] status is found in the project documents (nothing fabricated);
  - in the export rows, seed, numbers and intervals are identical to the engine;
  - the version is correct.

  `tests/dist/e2e.mjs` 168/168. The browser test covers:
  - all methods of the eight families on the sample data; distinguishing failed results on small plots;
  - the selected plot staying the same in all five tabs;
  - empty or missing data;
  - bootstrap and source sheets in Excel;
  - JSON;
  - the SVG metadata being identical to the parameters in the JSON;
  - load error (HTML instead of JavaScript) and no warning on a normal load.

  The unlabelled-control scan is clean in both languages in all steps and tabs.

### P16: stand models, PPM and PRM; NLS, SUR, log-OLS (11 Nov 2026; 2.1.0-beta.1)
Specification §4.1 and P16. Source: Poudel and Cao (2013) ([R1]) **[B]** for the model form and the PPM/PRM distinction. `dist/stand.js` (DOM-free), the "Stand model" workflow in the interface (Train model / Predict with model).

- **Training unit:** plot × period (the same grouping as `extractUnits`). The stand explanatory variables N (trees/ha), H (m), A (years) are read from columns of the tree table; each must be the same single positive number in all tree rows of the unit. An empty, unreadable, non-positive value, or one that varies within the unit, excludes the unit with a reason (no averaging, no estimation). Which column is H is the user's choice and is written to the model (Poudel's H is dominant/codominant height; the H0 of the Sample Plot tool is a different definition).
- **Responses:** PPM Weibull: b, c by maximum likelihood in the unit (a = 0). PRM Weibull Mk (k = 2…9): the targets of the method (mean, variance with n divisor, Dq, type 7 percentiles); if the location policy a = 0.5 × Dmin is chosen, Dmin is also a response (the source's policy), or the user's fixed a. M10 (which needs the n on the plot) is out of scope. PRM Parresol: mean, median, Dq; ξ from the user.
- **Model form:** y = exp(β₁ + β₂RS + β₃ ln N + β₄ ln H + β₅/A) + ε, RS = √(10000/N)/H; terms are selectable (intercept always). Error additive on the original scale.
- **NLS:** each response separately, Levenberg–Marquardt (`core/nls.js`), start log-OLS; asymptotic standard errors σ̂²(JᵀJ)⁻¹.
- **log-OLS:** least squares on ln y; a separate objective function (error multiplicative on the log scale). Back-transformation: exp(Zβ) predicts the median (default); under normal error on the log scale the mean is exp(Zβ + s²/2), s² = Σr²/(n − p) (standard lognormal result). The choice is written to the model.
- **SUR:** all responses jointly, Σᵢ rᵢᵀΣ⁻¹rᵢ is minimised. Feasible GLS: Σ from the equation-by-equation NLS residuals (n divisor); the residuals are whitened with W = L⁻¹ (Σ = LLᵀ) and all coefficients are solved jointly by LM; Σ is recomputed from the joint residuals and this is iterated until the relative coefficient change is < 10⁻¹⁰ (at most 100 rounds) (iterated SUR; a two-step option also exists). Cross-equation residual correlations are reported. A separate regression for each response is not called SUR.
- **Prediction:** only with a trained model file or one that has passed the schema check (`dist-stand-model/1`); there is no prediction with coefficients from papers. Checks (no clipping): predicted percentiles strictly increasing, Dq > mean, Dmin < smallest percentile; on violation no parameters are given, and the values are shown as predicted. In PRM the parameters are computed with the validated recovery methods (P06/P07, P09). N, H, A outside the training range are flagged as extrapolation.
- **Validation:** K-fold, assignment unit the plot (natural order, plot j → fold j mod K; no randomness), all periods of a plot in the same fold. In each fold the model is trained only on the training folds; for each held-out unit, the −ln L of its trees under the predicted distribution (total and per tree) and KS D. Units that cannot be predicted (inconsistent response, no recovery solution, nomodel) and units whose trees fall outside the predicted support (ln L = −∞) are counted.
- **Model file:** schema, tool version, response set, approach, terms, method and options, coefficients (and NLS standard errors, SUR Σ, correlations, number of rounds), units, H column, training coverage (units, number of plots, ranges of explanatory variables and targets), units that did not enter training, validation (K, summary, fold assignment), training units.
- **Finding:** in the sample data, when PRM Weibull M2 is used with a = 0.5 × predicted Dmin, in 10 of 54 units the predicted location is larger than the smallest trees of the unit; the likelihood of these units is zero and they are counted separately in the validation (this does not happen in PPM since a = 0 there).
- **Validation (test):** `tests/ref/make_dist_p16.py` generates the simulated training data with a fixed seed (the tool's sample file `assets/lab/dist/ornek/ornek-mescere.csv`: 36 plots, 54 units, 1926 trees) and computes independently with NumPy/SciPy: unit targets (Weibull ML profile equation with brentq, checked to be no worse than `weibull_min.fit`; moments; `numpy.quantile` linear), NLS (`least_squares`), log-OLS (`lstsq`), iterated SUR, plot-fold validation (`weibull_min.logpdf`, `kstest`), M2 recovery (brentq). `tests/dist/test_p16.mjs` 91/91: β's against the reference (2·10⁻⁵ relative; log-OLS 10⁻¹¹), SSE not larger than the reference optimum, Σ, the GLS objective of SUR being smaller than at the NLS coefficients, per-unit validation −ln L and KS (10⁻⁵), units outside the support, periods staying in the same fold, determinism, prediction checks and no clipping, extrapolation, recovery identical to the validated methods, log-OLS back-transformation, rejection of training with a single plot, rejection of prediction without a model, model file fields and import rejection (schema, coefficients). `tests/dist/e2e.mjs` 179/179 (sample data, NLS and SUR training, validation summary, model file download and reload, prediction = engine, extrapolation warning, phone).

### P17: CDFR and modified CDFR (11 Nov 2026; 2.1.0)
Specification §4.2 and P17. Source: Poudel and Cao (2013) ([R1]) **[B]**. `dist/stand.js` (`trainCDF`, `cdfObjective`, `unitClasses`), training in a Web Worker (`dist/stand-worker.js`).

- **What is optimised:** the regression coefficients β shared by all plots (the model form of P16). θᵢ(β) are the predicted parameters of the unit: in PPM b, c directly; in PRM (Weibull M2–M9) recomputed at every evaluation from the predicted targets with the validated recovery methods (P06/P07).
- **CDFR (source):** Σᵢ (1/nᵢ) Σⱼ [(j − 0.5)/nᵢ − F(dᵢ₍ⱼ₎; θᵢ)]². Diameters are sorted; equal diameters take consecutive j (not stated in the source; tool convention, written to the model).
- **Modified CDFR (source):** Σᵢ 1/(mᵢ − 1) Σ_{j=1}^{mᵢ−1} [Cᵢⱼ − F(uᵢⱼ; θᵢ)]². Classes of width w start at ⌊min/w⌋·w (w from the user; the source's 2 cm is the default); uᵢⱼ is the actual upper class bound (instead of "midpoint + 1" in the source), Cᵢⱼ the observed cumulative proportion. The last class is dropped **only from this objective function**; it stays in the class table and in the total number of trees (the `unitClasses` counts equal n). A unit with mᵢ = 1 cannot enter this objective function and is recorded with the reason.
- **M1 location policy:** in PPM a = 0 or a = 0.5 × predicted Dmin. In the latter, the unit targets b, c are computed by maximum likelihood with a = 0.5 × the unit's smallest diameter fixed, and Dmin becomes a response. In CDFR the Dmin equation (also in PRM) is estimated beforehand by NLS and held fixed; only the other equations are optimised by CDFR. The policy is written to the model.
- **Nested solvers:** if at some β the recovery of any unit fails or gives multiple solutions, that β is deemed infeasible; the step is rejected and counted (`rejected`). Multiple solutions are not selected by a rule (`rootPolicy`). If there is a failed unit at the NLS start, the model is not trained and the failures are counted by status (e.g. M8: multiple solutions in 54 of 54 units).
- **Solver:** Levenberg–Marquardt on the residual vector; Jacobian by forward differences column by column (at each β the unit parameters are computed once); convergence criteria the same as `core/nls.js`. Start NLS coefficients; initial and final objective value and number of iterations are recorded. No standard error is given.
- **Reproduction of the source and extensions:** the two objective functions implemented are those in the source (weights and positions unchanged; only the upper bound with the general definition). A truncated CDF or other weighting was not implemented; if added it will be defined separately under the name "extended CDFR".
- **Validation:** the plot-level cross-validation of P16 is used as is (CDFR is retrained in each fold).
- **Validation (test):** `tests/dist/test_p17.mjs` 58/58. Two hand-computed objective values: two units, CDFR (with equal diameters) and modified CDFR (with 2 cm and 4 cm classes). `tests/ref/make_dist_p17.py` independently writes the same objective functions and optimisation with NumPy/SciPy (`least_squares`, numerical Jacobian; M2 recovery brentq). The test compares:
  - the objective value at the reference's initial and optimal coefficients (10⁻¹⁰);
  - our optimum (within 10⁻⁶ of the reference, not worse than it) and the coefficients (2·10⁻³).

  Examples: PPM CDFR, modified CDFR (w = 2 and 4), PPM a = 0.5·Dmin, PRM M2 CDFR and modified CDFR (w = 3).

  The test also checks:
  - the Dmin equation coming from NLS and staying fixed;
  - rejection and counting of M8 due to multiple solutions;
  - exclusion of a single-class unit;
  - cross-validation with modified CDFR;
  - the model file.

  `tests/dist/e2e.mjs` 180/180: modified CDFR in a Web Worker, objective value identical to the engine.

### P18: L-moments, general percentile matching (QME), minimum distance (MGE) (12 Nov 2026; 2.2.0-beta.1)
Specification P18 and the §1.1 method matrix (L-moments for Weibull and gamma; general QME/MGE for gamma, normal and beta with fixed bounds). Source: the general framework the fitdistrplus documentation ([R8]) **[D]**, not read; lmomco ([R13]) in the specification **[D]** not read and not used (see below). `dist/fit/general.js`.

- **Default:** none of these eight methods is selected by default (`optional`). Since they do not maximise the likelihood they do not enter the AIC/BIC comparison; in the comparison table they are among the descriptive fits. A failed fit does not fall back to another method (status and reason, no parameters).
- **Sample L-moments:** unbiased, from probability-weighted moments: b0 = mean, b1 = (1/n) Σ (j − 1)/(n − 1) x₍ⱼ₎; ℓ1 = b0, ℓ2 = 2b1 − b0.
- **weibull.lmom** (a fixed, y = d − a): since λ2/λ1 = 1 − 2^(−1/c), c = −ln 2 / ln(1 − ℓ2/ℓ1), b = ℓ1/Γ(1 + 1/c) (the formula in the specification). If there is a diameter with y ≤ 0, no result is given.
- **gamma.lmom:** λ2/λ1 = Γ(k + ½)/(√π Γ(k + 1)), decreasing in k; the equation is solved exactly for k (Brent, on ln k, k ∈ [10⁻⁴, 10⁸]), θ = ℓ1/k. The rational approximation used by lmomco was not used because its coefficients were not read from the source; the exact equation was used (the difference between the two solutions is of the size of the approximation error). The formula was verified by numerical integration.
- **QME:** Σ wᵢ [Q(pᵢ; θ) − q̂ᵢ]² is minimised; q̂ the type 7 sample percentile; probabilities (default 0.1, 0.25, 0.5, 0.75, 0.9) strictly increasing, within (0, 1) and at least as many as the free parameters; weights positive and as many as the probabilities (default equal). If the selected percentiles do not determine 2 parameters (equal sample percentiles), no result is given. The probabilities, weights and percentile definition are written to the result.
- **MGE:** the selected distance is minimised: KS D, Cramér–von Mises W² or Anderson–Darling A² (`gof.js` definitions; default CvM). The criterion is written to the result and to the method name. The comparison table marks an MGE row with † in the column of the criterion it was fitted with ("fitted with and evaluated with the same criterion").
- **QME and MGE solver:** Nelder–Mead (`core/optim.js`), on the logarithm of positive parameters, start the moment estimate (beta: moments with fixed L, U). Stopping: F spread ≤ 10⁻¹³ (relative) and x spread ≤ 10⁻⁹. A tighter x tolerance could not be met because of floating-point ties on the flat bottom of the objective function (stalling at the optimum in 20 000 iterations in beta QME; finding).
- **Validation:** `tests/ref/make_dist_p18.py` independently computes with NumPy/SciPy:
  - sample L-moments, from the combinatorial definition;
  - theoretical λ2, as ∫ x(2F − 1)f dx with `quad`;
  - L-moment fits (brentq);
  - QME (`least_squares`, `ppf`);
  - MGE (`kstest`, `cramervonmises`, A² from logcdf/logsf; Nelder–Mead and Powell, five starts, the best).

  The reference uses two simulated samples (gamma, 60 trees; shifted Weibull, 45 trees).

  `tests/dist/test_p18.mjs` 71/71. Compared:
  - ℓ1, ℓ2: 10⁻¹²;
  - λ2 formulas: 10⁻⁹;
  - L-moment parameters: 10⁻¹⁰;
  - QME parameters: 2·10⁻⁶; objective value not larger than the reference optimum;
  - MGE minima: the same as the reference (10⁻⁴), not larger than it.

  The test also checks:
  - the recorded settings;
  - the three definitions (L-moment, moment, ML) giving different results;
  - no fallback to another method on failures (location, probability order and count, weight, non-identifiability, bounds, support);
  - the methods staying outside the AIC table.

  `tests/dist/e2e.mjs` 184/184: not selected by default; when probabilities, weights and criterion are given through the interface, the result is identical to the engine; the † mark.

### P19: Johnson SB four-parameter recovery, Fonseca, Marques and Parresol (2009) (12 Nov 2026; 2.2.0-beta.2)
Source: Fonseca, T.F., Marques, C.P., Parresol, B.R. 2009. Describing maritime pine diameter distributions with Johnson's SB distribution using a new all-parameter recovery approach. *Forest Science* 55(4): 367–373 ([R17]) **[B]** (PDF uploaded by the user, 12 Nov 2026; Equations 1–11, Appendix A1–A16 read). Parresol et al. (2009, SAS programs and constraint design), cited by the source, not read; the work could not be identified and is not in the bibliography (22 Nov 2026). `dist/recover/fonseca.js`, method `johnsonsb.fonseca` (kind `recover`, data type `summary`), the fourth tab of recovery from summaries in the interface. The Parresol (2003) method (`johnsonsb.parresol`, ξ from the user) remains unchanged.

- **Inputs:** median d₀.₅₀, mean d̄ (m₁), Dq (m₂ = Dq²) and the basal-area-weighted mean diameter d̄_G = Σgᵢdᵢ/Σgᵢ (source; m₃ = d̄_G·Dq², in the source with reference to Gove and Patil 1998, original not obtainable (22 Nov 2026, user decision; see the legend)). m₃ is **not derived** from the mean and variance; without d̄_G no computation is done. From raw data: mean, type 7 median, Dq and d̄_G = Σd³/Σd² (g ∝ d²).
- **Equations (source):** γ = δ ln(1/y₅₀ − 1), y₅₀ = (d₀.₅₀ − ξ)/λ (Equation 6, eliminates γ); ξ + λμ′₁ = d̄ (7); ξ² + 2ξλμ′₁ + λ²μ′₂ = Dq² (9, G = kN·Dq²); ξ³ + 3ξ²λμ′₁ + 3ξλ²μ′₂ + λ³μ′₃ = d̄_G·Dq² (11). μ′ᵣ = E[Yʳ] (Equation 10), with the `sbMomentsY` quadrature; m₃ was added at this stage: E[Y³] = c³ + 3c²e₁ + 3c·e₂ + e₃ (the same centred quadrature, c = the median of Y).
- **Region and solver (tool conventions; the source's constraints are in another publication):**
  - Region: 0 ≤ ξ < d₀.₅₀ (diameters positive), λ > d₀.₅₀ − ξ, δ > 0. The region is satisfied by construction: ξ = d₀.₅₀·σ(a), λ = (d₀.₅₀ − ξ)(1 + eᵇ), δ = eᶜ.
  - Solver: Newton with step halving on scaled residuals (moment/target − 1), numerical Jacobian. The source uses three-dimensional Levenberg–Marquardt; it is a different solver of the same equations.
