  - 48 fixed starts are used. Convergence: residual norm ≤ 10⁻¹². Distinct solutions are separated by a relative difference of 10⁻⁶.
- **Checks:**
  - Moment admissibility: Dq > d̄ and d̄_G > Dq²/d̄ (for a positive variable m₃m₁ > m₂²).
  - Symmetric input: rejected if |d₀.₅₀ − d̄| ≤ 10⁻⁹·Dq. Source: for a symmetric distribution the method reduces to three equations.
  - Number of solutions: one solution → ok; more than one → "multiple solutions" (candidates listed, no parameters); none → did not converge.
  - Conditioning: if the Jacobian condition number at the solution (from the eigenvalues of JᵀJ, Jacobi rotations) exceeds 10⁸, it is deemed weakly identified and no single result is given.
  - Back-calculation: median, mean, Dq and d̄_G must be satisfied within 10⁻⁹.
- **Finding:** when approaching symmetry, the condition number grows roughly as 1/γ. In the reference, in scaled original parameters, 6·10⁴, 6·10⁵, 6·10⁶ for γ = 0.1, 0.01, 0.001. Nearly symmetric inputs (around γ ≤ 10⁻⁴) are either deemed weakly identified or do not converge from any start; in both cases no single result is given. With summaries rounded to 0.01 cm (from ξ = 4, λ = 40, γ = 0.8, δ = 1.4) the solution becomes ξ = 4.09, λ = 39.63, γ = 0.785, δ = 1.386: the effect of rounding on ξ is marked.
- **Validation:** `tests/ref/make_dist_p19.py` computes the Equation 10 integrals with mpmath at 30 digits and solves independently (SciPy `least_squares`, 18 starts). Cases covered:
  - summaries from three known asymmetric parameter sets;
  - summaries rounded to 0.01 cm;
  - simulated raw data (400 trees; d̄_G = Σg·d/Σg);
  - condition numbers for γ → 0.

  `tests/dist/test_p19.mjs` 32/32. Compared:
  - d̄_G = m₃/m₂: 10⁻¹²;
  - recovery of the true parameters: 10⁻⁹; against the reference solution: 10⁻⁷;
  - single solution, back-calculation and the recorded m₂, m₃;
  - rounded and raw data cases: 10⁻⁶ against the reference solution;
  - targets from raw data: 10⁻¹².

  The test also checks:
  - symmetric rejection and the growth of the condition number in both implementations;
  - no single result for nearly symmetric inputs;
  - no computation without d̄_G, rejection of inadmissible moments and of Dq ≤ mean;
  - the solution lying within the region.

  `tests/dist/e2e.mjs` 188/188: four parameters in the interface, the m₃ row, [R17], condition number, the median shared with the Parresol tab, rejection of symmetric input.

### P20: Generalised gamma and Gumbel (maximum form) (12 Nov 2026; 2.2.0-beta.3)
Sources: SciPy `scipy.stats.gengamma` documentation ([R18], SciPy 1.18.0 manual; read on 22 Nov 2026) **[B]**: f(x, a, c) = |c| x^(ca−1) exp(−x^c)/Γ(a), with scale b the density here for c > 0 (SciPy also allows c < 0, not covered here); NIST/SEMATECH e-Handbook §1.3.6.6.16, Extreme Value Type I ([R19]; read on 22 Nov 2026) **[B]**: the maximum form (mean μ + 0.5772β, SD βπ/√6) is the one here, the minimum form is listed separately there. The equations are the definitions in the specification (DIST_SPEC P20) and were verified with an independent numerical reference. As the specification requires, two separate changes: first the generalised gamma, then Gumbel. All methods of the two families are **optional** (`optional`, not selected by default).

**Generalised gamma** (`dist/families/gengamma.js`, `dist/fit/gengamma.js`; family `gengamma`):
- f(d) = c/[bΓ(a)]·(d/b)^(ac − 1)·exp[−(d/b)^c], d > 0; a > 0 shape, b > 0 scale (cm), c > 0 shape. F(d) = P(a, (d/b)^c); Q_p = b·[P⁻¹(a, p)]^(1/c); E[Dʳ] = bʳΓ(a + r/c)/Γ(a). Variance in the form E[D]²·expm1(L₂ − 2L₁) (L_r = ln E[Dʳ]).
- c = 1 is the two-parameter gamma (shape a, scale b), a = 1 the two-parameter Weibull (shape c, scale b). The a here is a shape, not the Weibull location parameter; there is no location (it is not a three-parameter shifted gamma). Only c > 0 is covered.
- **`gengamma.ml`:** since D^c is distributed as a two-parameter gamma (shape a, scale b^c), the maximum likelihood estimate of (a, b) with c fixed is the gamma ML on d^c. The likelihood is profiled over c (ỹ = ln d − ȳ):
  - s(c) = ln mean(e^(cỹ)); a(c) is the root of ln a − ψ(a) = s(c) (the same Brent and shape interval [10⁻⁴, 10⁸] as `gamma.ml`); ln b(c) = ȳ + [s(c) − ln a(c)]/c;
  - ℓp(c) = n[a ln a − a − lnΓ(a) − a·s(c) + ln c] − nȳ; its derivative (envelope theorem) is the score in c: dℓp/dc = n[1/c − a(c)·w(c)], w(c) = Σỹe^(cỹ)/Σe^(cỹ);
  - tool convention: ln c grid (121 points, 0.01 ≤ c ≤ 100), root of the score between the neighbours of the largest grid point by Brent (10⁻¹⁴, ln c);
  - if the maximum is at the edge of the grid, `boundary` (towards the c → 0 lognormal limit; seen in samples where ln d is right-skewed), no parameters given; also `boundary` if a(c) is out of range; if there are multiple local maxima on the grid a warning (`gg.multimodal`) is written and the largest is given; at least three distinct diameters are required.
- Threshold (`gengamma.tml`) and classed data (`gengamma.gml`): the P11 and P12 infrastructure (Nelder–Mead, a, b, c on the log scale), start `gengamma.ml`.
- In the comparison table, if there is a model with a different number of free parameters greater than 2, a note: an extra parameter does not decrease ln L in nested models; a more flexible family is not automatically a better biological model.

**Gumbel, maximum form** (`dist/families/gumbel.js`, `dist/fit/gumbel.js`; family `gumbel`):
- F(d) = exp{−exp[−(d − μ)/β]}, μ location (cm), β > 0 scale (cm); f = (1/β)e^(−z)exp(−e^(−z)), z = (d − μ)/β; Q_p = μ − β ln(−ln p); E[D] = μ + γ_E β (γ_E = 0.5772156649…), Var = π²β²/6. Right-skewed. The minimum form (F = 1 − exp{−exp[(d − μ)/β]}, mean μ − γ_E β) is a different distribution and is not offered. ln(1 − F) below the median log1p(−F), above it ln(−expm1(−t)), in the extreme tail −z − t/2 (t = e^(−z)).
- The support is all real numbers: **the probability of a negative diameter F(0) = exp[−exp(μ/β)]** is reported in every result (including thresholded and classed fits, the F(0) of the fitted model).
- **`gumbel.ml`:** β = d̄ − Σd e^(−d/β)/Σe^(−d/β), μ = −β ln[(1/n)Σe^(−d/β)]. h(β) = β − d̄ + weighted mean, its derivative 1 + var_w(d)/β² > 0, at β → 0 d_min − d̄ < 0: single root; weights overflow-free with e^(−(d − d_min)/β), Brent in an interval expanded from the moment estimate.
- **`gumbel.mom`:** β = √(6v)/π, μ = d̄ − γ_E β; v divisor n (default) or n − 1.
- Threshold (`gumbel.tml`): conditional likelihood Σ ln f − n ln[F(U) − F(L)] (P11); classed data (`gumbel.gml`, P12). The untruncated equations are not used on thresholded data.

**Validation:** `tests/ref/make_dist_p20.py` → `tests/ref/dist_p20.json`.
- Generalised gamma: functions mpmath 40 digits (from the definition; tails included), SciPy `gengamma` in the body; moments with mpmath quadrature; at c = 1 and a = 1 SciPy `gamma` and `weibull_min` and the engine's own gamma and Weibull families; ML: Nelder–Mead on the full likelihood (four starts) followed by the three score equations with mpmath `findroot` at 30 digits, `gengamma.fit` cross-check; ln L values of the nested gamma and Weibull ML; in a sample where ln d is right-skewed, the profile at fixed c (two score equations, mpmath) increases as c decreases; threshold (L = 8 cm) and classed (2 cm) likelihoods by Nelder–Mead.
- Gumbel: functions mpmath 40 digits, SciPy `gumbel_r` in the body; mean, variance, F(0) from SciPy; ML score equations at 30 digits, `gumbel_r.fit` cross-check; moments by `fsolve` on `gumbel_r.stats` (not the closed form); threshold (8 ≤ d ≤ 40) and classed likelihoods by Nelder–Mead.
- `tests/dist/test_p20.mjs` 848/848: functions 10⁻¹¹ (gengamma) and 10⁻¹³ (Gumbel); ML parameters against the score root 10⁻⁷ (gengamma) and 10⁻¹² (Gumbel), ln L 10⁻¹²; at c = 1 profile = gamma ML ln L; gengamma ln L ≥ gamma and Weibull ML; multimodal profile warning; `boundary` at the lower edge of c; threshold and classed ln L 10⁻⁹ (gengamma) and 10⁻¹⁰ (Gumbel); reporting of the probability that Gumbel assigns to negative diameters in a sample with positive diameters.
- `tests/dist/e2e.mjs` 194/194: the two families are offered under their explicit names but are not selected by default; the parameters in the interface are identical to the engine; the number-of-parameters note in the AIC table; F(0) in the Gumbel notes. The per-family method counts in the P11/P12 tests exclude the optional families.

### P23: Empirical basal area diameter distribution, descriptive (12 Nov 2026; 2.2.0)
Source: Kangas, A., Maltamo, M. 2000. Percentile based basal area diameter distribution models for Scots pine, Norway spruce and birch species. *Silva Fennica* 34(4): 371–380 ([R23]) **[B]** (Sections 2.2–2.3, Equation 1). `dist/basal.js`; in the interface the "Basal area diameter distribution (descriptive)" card in the "Classes" tab; in Excel the "BA distribution" sheet. Only with raw diameters; not given for classified frequencies.
- g = πd²/40000 (m², d cm). F_N(x) = #{dᵢ ≤ x}/n and F_G(x) = Σ_{dᵢ ≤ x} gᵢ / Σgᵢ, drawn together (step functions).
- **Percentiles (tool convention, written in the result):** x_p = the smallest observed diameter satisfying F_G(d) ≥ p (inverse of the weighted step function; p = 0 → smallest diameter); tolerance 10⁻¹² for floating-point sums. p = 0, 0.1, …, 0.9, 0.95, 1 (specification). dgM = x₀.₅. The prediction model from 12 percentiles in the source and the Späth rational spline interpolation were not implemented (specification: only if a workflow for building a distribution from percentiles is added).
- **Classes:** the class definition is that of the "Classes" tab. b_k = Σ_{class} g × 10000/area (m²/ha; without an area, m² on the plot), share = Σ_{class} g / Σg. Conversion to number of trees f_k = b_k/ḡ_k (Equation 1): if ḡ_k is the mean basal area of the trees in the class, f_k equals the observed count (per hectare) exactly; if it is the basal area of the class mid-diameter, it is labelled approximate and not given for an open class. Trees below, above and in the gaps of the classes are in separate rows.
- No parametric family is fitted to basal-area-weighted data; size-biased likelihood, angle-count data and the species coefficients of the source are out of scope. The class tables and fits by number of trees do not change.
- **Validation:** `tests/ref/make_dist_p23.py` (NumPy: `numpy.unique` and cumulative sums, `numpy.percentile(…, weights = g, method = 'inverted_cdf')`, `numpy.digitize`), four samples (fixture w2, w3, small; a sample with tied diameters), definitions with tails, unequal widths and open classes. `tests/dist/test_p23.mjs` 257/257: hand-computed example (d = 10, 20, 30: F_G = 1/14, 5/14, 1; dgM = 30; mid-diameter approximation 177.78 trees/ha), F_N and F_G 10⁻¹³, percentiles identical to NumPy, b_k, share, ḡ_k, f_k; Σb_k = G, Σf_k = N, sum of shares 1; class counts identical to the number-of-trees table. `tests/dist/e2e.mjs` 200/200: percentiles and dgM in the interface identical to the engine, the two cumulative distributions are drawn, the definition is written, with an area Σ G (m²/ha) and Σ f = N/ha, the number-of-trees class table does not change, Excel sheet, no horizontal scroll on a phone.

**End of the revision (12 Nov 2026, user decision):** P21 (Burr XII, Dagum) and P22 (two-component mixtures) were left out of scope; the diameter distribution revision was completed with P23 as **2.2.0**.

### P24: Weibull three-parameter maximum likelihood, location estimated (23 Nov 2026; 2.3.0-beta.1)
Source: ForestFit, `fitWeibull` help page (R package by M. Teimouri, version 2.4.3, read 23 Nov 2026) ([R6]) **[P]**: the two- and three-parameter Weibull with location θ (here a), scale β (b) and shape α (c), f(x) = (c/b)((x − a)/b)^(c−1) exp{−((x − a)/b)^c}, x > a; `location = TRUE` with the ML method (the help page names it "mle", the code of 2.4.3 accepts "ml"; −ℓ minimised by `optim` Nelder–Mead from the starts). Test reference = the package's output (`tests/ref/dist_p24_ml3.R`, best of nine starts).
- **Method `weibull.ml3`** (unchecked by default): profile likelihood ℓp(a) = max over b, c of ℓ(a, b, c), each ℓp(a) from the fixed-location ML of P05 (the score equation for c, b = (mean xᶜ)^(1/c), x = d − a), maximised over **0 ≤ a < Dmin** (tool constraint: a negative location has no meaning for diameters; ForestFit allows any real a). Solver: a grid of 48 points in a, denser next to Dmin (a = top·(1 − (1 − u)²), top = Dmin·(1 − 10⁻⁶)), then golden-section search in the bracket of the best grid point (|Δa| ≤ 10⁻¹²). k = 3 in AIC/BIC.
- **Boundary diagnostics** (DIST_SPEC §3.5: with free a the likelihood can be unbounded as a → Dmin, where c < 1 makes the density infinite at the smallest diameter): best grid point next to Dmin → status `boundary`, no parameters (`ml3.edge`); best location with c ≤ 1 → `boundary` (`ml3.cle1`); best at a = 0 → the two-parameter ML with a warning (`ml3.atzero`); more than one local maximum of ℓp on the grid → warning (`ml3.multimodal`). At least 4 trees (tool rule).
- **R code:** the method is written like the other ML fits (fitdistrplus `mle` with a, b, c free, starting from two significant digits of the tool's result); it is not yet among the cases of `scripts/rcode-check.sh`.
- **Validation:** `tests/dist/test_p24.mjs` (18) against ForestFit 2.4.3: two simulated samples (a = 5 and 10) and a sample with true a = 0: ℓ not below the package's, a within 0.01 cm, b and c within 10⁻³ (relative; the package stops at Nelder–Mead's default tolerance), a profile maximum inside (0, Dmin) with c > 1; the tool's sample data, where the package's optimum is a = −1.33: a = 0 and the two-parameter result; a sample with c < 1, where the package stops at a = Dmin: `boundary`.


### P25: Per-hectare stand table, descriptive (23 Nov 2026; 2.3.0-beta.2)
User decision (23 Nov 2026; DIST_SPEC decision 11): the number of trees per hectare by class over the plots of a period, in the Classes tab and the Excel sheet "Per hectare" (`dist/hatable.js`). Raw diameters with a plot column only.
- The estimation unit is the plot, as in Plot & Stand Metrics (§6, design-based estimate): y_ik = n_ik · 10000 / A_i (trees of plot i in class k, plot area A_i in m²; 0 where the plot has no tree in the class), ȳ_k = Σ y_ik / m, SE(ȳ_k) = s_k / √m with s_k² = Σ (y_ik − ȳ_k)² / (m − 1) (simple random sampling, Cochran 1977 §2 **[B]**, no finite population correction; one plot: no SE). Trees below, above or between the classes in their own row; the class means add up to the total. Plots of different sizes are not pooled as one area (the mean of the plots' values, not Σn / ΣA). Plots without any tree are not in the data and are not counted (stated on screen).
- **Plot area:** one area for every plot (the existing "Plot area" field) or a column holding each plot's area (m²; the same on every row of a plot, otherwise an error naming the plots; a plot without a positive area: error).
- **All plots together** (the pooled unit): the per-hectare factor of the class table, the error index EI and the basal area table is 10000 / (sum of the plot areas of the period); before 2.3.0-beta.2 it was 10000 / (one plot's area), which overstated N/ha by the number of plots.
- No distribution is fitted to these numbers (DIST_SPEC decision 3 stays: no hectare-weighted fitting).
- **Validation:** `tests/ref/make_dist_p25.py` (NumPy, from the definition: `numpy.digitize`, `std(ddof = 1)/√m`), five plots of 250–1000 m²; `tests/dist/test_p25.mjs` (9): means and SE to 1e-9, outside and total rows, the sum, the difference from the pooled ratio, one plot, area conflicts and missing areas. `tests/dist/e2e.mjs` §2u: a pasted table with an area column (A 400 m², B 200 m²: 12.5 and 75/ha, total 87.5 ± 12.5) and with a common area; §2j: the pooled table of three 400 m² plots gives 208.3/ha (was 625).

### P26: Replication of Poudel and Cao (2013): method 10, backward elimination, method comparison (23 Nov 2026; 2.3.0-beta.3)
User request (23 Nov 2026; DIST_SPEC decision 12): the tool should let a user repeat the evaluation of Poudel and Cao (2013) ([R1]) **[B]** with their own data. `dist/stand.js` (method 10, per-equation terms, `eliminate`) and `dist/standcmp.js` (comparison; DOM-free, run in `stand-worker.js`).
- **Method 10 as a stand-model response set** (`prm.weibull.m10`): responses Dq, Q25, Q50, Q95 and Dmin; n is not a response but the number of trees measured on the unit (training) or entered with N, H and A (prediction). The location a = (n^(1/3)·Dmin − Q50)/(n^(1/3) − 1) from the predicted Dmin and Q50 (P06 recovery; a negative a is reported, `m10.aneg`, never moved to zero). In CDFR and modified CDFR the Dmin and Q50 equations are fitted by NLS beforehand and held fixed (source appendix: d0 and d50 come from their own equations), the other equations are fitted by the CDF objective.
- **Per-equation terms and backward elimination** (source: "removing insignificant variables (at the 5% level)"): for each response, NLS with the candidate terms; the term (never the intercept) with the largest two-sided p of t = β/SE (Student's t, df = n − p, asymptotic NLS standard errors) is dropped while p > α (default 0.05) and the equation refitted. The selected terms are then used by NLS, SUR (equations with different designs in the stacked whitened problem), CDFR and modified CDFR (start values); not with log-OLS. The steps (response, term, p) are listed with the model and kept in the model file (`equations[].terms`); older model files without them still load.
- **Statistics of a validation unit** under its predicted Weibull (source Eqs. 6–9): AD and KS as in §12 gof; mLogL = −Σ ln f(dⱼ); EI = Σₖ |nₖ·N/n − N·[F(upperₖ) − F(lowerₖ)]| in trees per hectare over classes of width w (default 2 cm, from ⌊min/w⌋·w), N the unit's trees/ha, n its trees measured. A unit with a tree below the predicted location (likelihood zero) is left out for that method and counted ("tree below the predicted location"), as are units without a model, inconsistent predictions and recoveries without a unique solution, each with its reason.
- **Splits** (tool convention; the source drew separate fit and validation plots from a larger study and repeated the evaluation on 10 samples): the plots are shuffled with mulberry32(seed + r) and the first round(share·m) fit the models, the others validate; all periods of a plot stay on one side; every method sees the same R splits (default R = 10, share 0.5, seed 1).
- **Summary and ranks:** per method the mean and SD of each statistic over the validation units of all splits. Default: only the units evaluated by every ranked method (common units, as the common rows of Model Evaluation), so all methods are judged on the same trees; option: each method's own units. Relative rank per statistic (source Eq. 10, smaller is better; `core/relrank.js`), overall rank = relative rank of the sum of the four ranks (source Table 3 note). Methods can be excluded from the ranks (the source left out SUR methods 4 and 5) without refitting; EI enters only when every ranked method has it. Results: table (mean (SD), rank per statistic, units, overall rank, failures), radar chart of the relative ranks, CSV of the summary and of the per-split means.
- **Not covered:** the R code generator (methods 1–9 with shared terms only; disabled with a note otherwise); the comparison runs in the browser and can take minutes for many methods × CDF fits (progress and cancel shown).
- **Validation:** `tests/ref/make_dist_p26.py` (SciPy, from the definitions: `weibull_min` logcdf/logsf/logpdf, `kstest`, EI by class; backward elimination with `least_squares` NLS, SE from σ²(JᵀJ)⁻¹, `t.sf`) on the simulated sample; `tests/dist/test_p26.mjs` (36): AD, KS, mLogL, EI of four units with given parameters (1e-8), elimination steps, p-values (1e-5) and reduced coefficients (1e-6) for the method-2 responses and Dmin, per-equation prediction and model file, SUR with equal per-equation terms = shared SUR, the method-10 location from the predicted Dmin and Q50 on every unit, CDFR method 10 with Dmin and Q50 fixed from NLS, split determinism and disjointness, a hand check of the relative and overall ranks, exclusion, common units and the mean over them. `tests/dist/e2e.mjs` §2v: the comparison tab on the sample (2 splits, SUR and NLS × methods 1 and 2) equals the engine (mean KS, overall rank), radar, exclusion, CSV, phone width.

## 13. Shared infrastructure of the new tools (TOOLS_SPEC T01, 12 Nov 2026)
Shared parts of the section volume, site index, stand structure, biomass and taper tools (`docs/TOOLS_SPEC.md`). Contains no scientific computation; the data rules are tested with hand-built cases.

**Coefficient record `model-coef/1`** (`core/coef.js`). The site contains no species or regional coefficients (K1). Each tool registers the equation **forms** it supports (`registerForm`: id, tool, parameter names, input and output units, bark status, the formula shown on screen, the scale it is written on: linear, ln or log10); the user only enters coefficients for a registered form, no free formula is written. Validation (`validateCoef`) rejects the following cases with separate codes: schema, id, tool, unknown form or form belonging to another tool, missing/extra/non-numeric coefficient, input or output without unit, invalid bark status (`ob`/`ib`), validity range with minimum ≥ maximum, transformation not matching the scale of the form (a log10 form is never read as ln), correction without a value, **empty source reference**, invalid origin (only `user-entered` and `fitted`; there is no "built-in" origin), a `fitted` set without a fit summary.
- Back-transformation correction (`correctionFactor`): Baskerville exp(SEE²/2), SEE on the natural logarithm scale; for an equation written with log10, first converted to SEE·ln 10. Snowdon ratio and user factor as they are. Source: Baskerville (1972); Sprugel (1983) **[B]** (PDF provided by the user, read on 22 Nov 2026: CF = exp(SEE²/2) with SEE from natural logarithms; a base-10 SEE is converted by multiplying by ln 10 = 2.303); Snowdon (1991) **[B]** (PDF provided by the user, read on 22 Nov 2026: the ratio of the arithmetic sample mean to the mean of the back-transformed predictions; the tool takes the value from the source, no formula). BC01 (16 Nov 2026): Baskerville on the ln scale [A] (§22); log₁₀ conversion (Sprugel) and Snowdon [D].
- Validity range (`outsideValid`): inputs outside the range are listed; tools compute and warn (TOOLS_SPEC §1.1).
- Storage: in the browser with `coef:<id>` keys (`core/store.js`, with the new listing function `keys(prefix)`); JSON library file `model-coef-library/1` (export/import; numbers are preserved bit for bit in JSON; invalid sets and sets belonging to another tool are not silently dropped, they are reported with their problems).
- Editor (`core/coef-ui.js`): form selection, coefficients (decimal point only, exponential notation such as 1.2e-5 accepted; a value with a comma or an unreadable value is rejected, not guessed), units (from the form), validity range, correction, species/region/component, source (reference required; DOI, page, table, note). Unpublished test page `tests/core/coef-ui.html`.

**Stem measurement table `stem-sections/1`** (`core/stems.js`, `readStems`). One row = one measurement point of one tree. Internal units: h m (**from the ground**), d cm, double bark thickness mm (`core/num.js` `UNITS.bark`; for age `UNITS.age`). If there are two crossed diameters, the combination rule is a setting: arithmetic mean (default) or √((d₁² + d₂²)/2) (equal area). Bark: table default or per row `ob`/`ib`; if there is a bark thickness, d_ib = d − thickness/10. A point measured inside bark carries only d_ib and is not mixed with the outside-bark diameter. Tree-level fields (dbh, height, cutting height) come from the table itself or from a tree list (`forest-dataset/1` trees); the list is joined **before the checks**.
- Error (the tree is not computed; `state: 'error'`): unreadable or missing value, negative height, zero/negative diameter, diameter thinner than the bark, invalid bark code, repeated height in the same tree, measurement higher than the total height, cut higher than the height, single measurement point, conflicting dbh/height/cutting height between rows of the same tree or between the table and the tree list. Since losing one row would silently reduce the tree's volume, the whole tree is deemed in error.
- Warning (the tree is computed): diameter increasing with height outside the butt swell zone (default h < 1.3 m, setting); a difference greater than 10% between the diameter measured at 1.30 m and dbh (setting); outside-bark and inside-bark points in the same tree; a tree without a record in the tree list.
- Ids are text ("01" ≠ "1"); different periods of the same tree are separate records. Thresholds are tool conventions, not source values, and are written together with the result.

**Multiple-root scanner** `core/roots.js` `scanRoots(f, lo, hi, k, { grid })`: moved from the distribution engine (`dist/recover/weibull.js`) with unchanged behaviour (geometric grid + Brent; repeated roots merge at a relative difference of 10⁻⁹); a linear grid option was added for intervals starting from zero, such as height. The distribution engine calls the same function with its own interval (the P06, P07 tests passed unchanged).

**Tests:** `tests/core/test_t01.mjs` 88/88 (form registration, 18 invalid set cases, correction factors, validity range, JSON round trip, storage and listing, units, every error and warning case in the stem table with its row number, joining the tree list, periods, identity of the root scanner with the distribution engine); `tests/core/e2e.mjs` 22/22 (editor: rejection of a comma coefficient and of a set without a source, validity range, log form and Baskerville, editing, deleting, JSON download and upload, persistence on reload, English, phone width). The site tests (`test_cite`, `test_guide`, `e2e`) now read the number of tools and categories from `inc/site.php`.


## 14. Stem Section Volume (`assets/lab/secvol/calc.js`; TOOLS_SPEC §2)

### SV01: computational core (12 Nov 2026)
Input: one tree from the `stem-sections/1` table (§13): measurement points sorted by height above ground, outside-bark (d) and/or inside-bark (d_ib) diameters, total height H, cutting height, dbh. g(d) = (π/4)(d/100)² m², length m, volume m³. Sources: the Huber, Smalian and Newton formulas are textbook definitions (Avery and Burkhart 2002; Kershaw et al. 2016; West 2009) **[D], textbook-definition exception** (12 Nov 2026, user decision): they were allowed to be implemented without reading the source, because the formulas are standard geometric definitions (mean, end and Simpson rules for frustum and paraboloid segments) and were verified with SciPy integration on known solids. The exception applies only to these three formulas; it does not apply to equations with coefficients. Hohenadl method: Heger, L. 1965. A trial of Hohenadl's method of stem form and stem volume estimation. *The Forestry Chronicle* (December 1965): 466–475 **[B]** (PDF uploaded by the user, 12 Nov 2026; after Krenn and Prodan 1944). Source: V = (π/4)·0.2H·(d²₀.₁ + d²₀.₃ + … + d²₀.₉), diameters at 0.1H, 0.3H … 0.9H (from the top in the source; the same set of heights), five equal cylinders; true form quotients η_i = d_i/d₀.₉ and true cylindrical form factor λ₀.₉ = 0.2·Ση_i², V = (π/4)·d²₀.₉·H·λ₀.₉ (d₀.₉: 0.9H from the top = 0.1H from the ground). The implemented formula is the same as the source. Huber, Smalian and Newton remain **[D]** under the textbook-definition exception above.

| Method (id) | Volume | Accepted data layout |
|---|---|---|
| `huber` | g_m·L | `mids` (each point a section midpoint) or `triples` (the midpoints are used) |
| `smalian` | (g₁ + g₂)/2·L | `ends` (each point a section boundary) or `triples` (the ends are used) |
| `newton` | L/6·(g₁ + 4g_m + g₂) | `triples` (end, middle, end, middle, …, end) |
| `hohenadl` | (H/5)·Σ g(d₀.₁H, d₀.₃H, d₀.₅H, d₀.₇H, d₀.₉H) | points at 0.1H … 0.9H (tolerance 0.05 m, setting); the whole stem from ground to tip, no separate stump and top section |

- **The data layout is the user's choice**, the tool does not guess it. The specification listed two layouts (end and middle measurements); Newton needs an end and a middle in each section, so a third layout `triples` was added (an end–middle–end sequence; that the middle point is really in the middle is checked with a 0.01 m tolerance, setting).
- **Boundaries from middle measurements:** b₀ = cutting height (required), b(i+1) = 2·m(i) − b(i). If a middle is not above the previous boundary, or if the last derived boundary exceeds the total height, nothing is computed. Points of type `stump`, `dbh` and `top` do not count as middles; if there is a point of type `top` at the last boundary, it is the base diameter of the top section.
- **Stump:** a cylinder between the ground and the cutting height, with the measured diameter at the cutting height; it is a separate row and is added to the total only if `includeStump` is selected (default: not added). If there is no point at the cutting height, the stump is not computed; if the first point is above the cut, the gap between is reported, not filled.
- **Top section:** from the last boundary to the total height; cone gL/3 (default, tool convention K7), paraboloid gL/2, neiloid gL/4 (general form g(t) = g₀(1 − t/L)^r, r = 1, 2, 3; volume g₀L/(r + 1)). If there is no total height or no diameter at the last boundary, there is no top section and the volume is labelled "up to the last boundary".
- **Bark:** outside-bark and inside-bark volume are computed separately; if the relevant diameter is missing at all points of a tree, that volume is not computed (warning), not estimated.
- **Form factor:** f₁.₃ = V/(g₁.₃·H), breast-height form factor; whether V includes the stump (or, for Hohenadl, is from ground to tip) is written in the result. Not given if the volume is "up to the last boundary".
- **Merchantable volume (optional top diameter d*):** within the measured stem, the height of d* by **linear interpolation** in diameter between neighbouring points, the cut section computed with the same method and with linearly interpolated diameters; if d* is within the top section, with the selected solid's own profile: d(t) = d₀(1 − t/L)^(r/2), t* = L[1 − (d*/d₀)^(2/r)], volume g₀L/(r + 1)·[1 − (1 − t*/L)^(r+1)]. The stump is not added to the merchantable volume. The rule is written in the result.
- **Statuses:** an erroneous tree (`stem-sections/1` checks), a layout not matching the method, a missing Hohenadl point, measurements falling short of the total height are not computed; none of them is shown as zero or a partial number.

**Validation:** `tests/ref/make_secvol_sv01.py` (SciPy 1.18.1, NumPy 2.5.3): exact volume for paraboloid, cone and neiloid with `scipy.integrate.quad`; Huber, Smalian, Newton (one and four sections) and Hohenadl sums written separately from the definition; in the top section the height of d* by `brentq`, its volume by `quad`; on 40 simulated trees (unequal sections, not real measurements) three layouts, stump, three top solids and merchantable volume (`numpy.interp`). `tests/secvol/test_sv01.mjs` 949/949: TOOLS_SPEC §8.1 table, exactness of Newton for the cone and neiloid, Huber under / Smalian over behaviour, derived boundaries, outside/inside-bark distinction, form factor, all status codes, end to end from the table reader.

### SV02: interface, tree and plot level (12 Nov 2026)
Page `section-volume.php` (Tool 8, category "Tree and stem", version 1.0.0-beta.1), interface `assets/lab/secvol/ui.js`, texts `i18n.js`.
- **Data:** stem measurement table (import panel; columns are suggested from the headers, the user changes them); optional link to confirmed tree data (dbh and total height from there if missing, plot sizes, number of trees whose stems were not measured). Sample data `assets/lab/secvol/ornek/ornek-seksiyon.csv` (`tests/ref/make_secvol_sample.py`; simulation, not real measurements).
- **Settings:** data layout (ends, middles, end–middle–end, Hohenadl) and only the methods that fit that layout; top section solid; whether the stump is in the total; merchantable top diameter; expansion to per hectare (from a column, a single size, from the confirmed data); check thresholds under "Details".
- **Expansion to per hectare** (`plotTotals`): per plot × period Σ V × 10000/A, outside-bark and inside-bark separately; trees that could not be computed or (if confirmed data are linked) whose stems were not measured are counted, their volumes **are not filled in**; the status of such a plot is "trees missing". A bark state is summed only if it exists in all computed trees. When the plot size is read from a column, unreadable, non-positive or conflicting values for the same plot are reported with their row, and no size is given to that plot (`plotAreas`).
- **Multiple methods** (Huber, Smalian, Newton in the end–middle–end layout): volumes side by side; difference as a percentage relative to Newton (if Newton is not selected, relative to the first selected method); no method is declared "correct".
- **Outputs:** tree table, plot table, tree detail (section breakdown, profile plot: measured points and the profile of the top section according to the selected solid), issue list with row numbers; Excel (Info: tool, version, settings, thresholds, rules; Trees; Plots; Sections; Problems), CSV, JSON.
- **Validation:** `tests/secvol/e2e.mjs` 58/58: with trees of exact cone profile, the volumes, form factors, m³/ha and merchantable volume on screen identical to the engine (the engine was validated against SciPy in SV01); in the cone tree the top section is the exact cone volume; in a paraboloid top the 7 cm height according to the solid's profile; repeated height with its row number; method list according to the layout; in the end–middle–end layout Huber < Newton < Smalian; CSV/JSON/Excel; sample data (decimal comma suggestion); English; phone and dark theme. Guide section `seksiyon-hacmi` (TR/EN), label check in `tests/site/test_guide.mjs`.

### SV03: data quality and method comparison (12 Nov 2026)
- **Quality report** (`qualityReport`, per tree): number of measurement points; number of diameters increasing with height outside the butt swell zone (`stem-sections/1` check, §13); minimum, mean and maximum of the section lengths; share of the top section in the volume (from the outside-bark volume, otherwise inside-bark), warning if above **10%** (tool convention, setting: if the share is large, the volume is sensitive to the selected top solid); relative difference between the diameter measured at 1.30 m and dbh (threshold `dbhTol`, default 10%). The thresholds (butt swell zone, dbh difference, top share) are written in the result, in the Excel "Info" sheet and on screen.
- **Method comparison** (`methodComparison`, if more than one method was selected in the end–middle–end layout): per tree 100·(V_m − V_ref)/V_ref and the difference of the totals over **the trees computed with every method**; minimum, mean and maximum of the per-tree differences. The reference Newton (if not selected, the first selected method) is only the denominator; no method is declared "correct". Outside-bark volume, otherwise inside-bark; which one was used is written. The known behaviour (§14 SV01: equality for the paraboloid, exactness of Newton up to third degree, Huber under / Smalian over for the cone and neiloid) is explained under "Details".
- **Version** remained 1.0.0-beta.1: the computed volumes and the method set did not change, only the report was added (K4).
- **Validation:** `tests/secvol/test_sv03.mjs` 43/43: for three solids, the difference of Huber and Smalian relative to Newton (since Newton is exact, (V − V_exact)/V_exact) identical to the SciPy reference and to the TOOLS_SPEC §8.1 values, also end to end from the stem table for the cone; common trees, totals, change of reference, inside-bark case; quality flags (increasing diameter, large top share, dbh difference, tree not computable) and each of the three thresholds changing the result and being written to the record. `tests/secvol/e2e.mjs` 65/65 (quality summary and threshold change, comparison table, explanation).

### Audit fixes (12 Nov 2026; 1.0.0-beta.2)
- **Cutting height and stump:** in the "ends" and "end–middle–end" layouts, if there is a measurement point below the cutting height (e.g. on the ground, h = 0) and there is also a point at the cutting height, the sections start from the cutting height; the points below belong only to the stump (`base.split` warning). Previously the part between 0 and the cut could be counted both as a section and as stump (twice when the stump was included). If there is no point at the cutting height and the sections start below it, the stump cannot be separated and this is written (`base.below`). The "middles" layout already started from the cut.
- **Hohenadl output:** the true form quotients η and λ₀.₉ (Heger 1965) in the result and in the tree detail; merchantable volume by top diameter is not given (`merch.hoh`, there is no profile to cut in five equal sections), and the top diameter field is not shown in this layout.
- **Merchantable volume:** every case where it cannot be computed (`merch.hoh`, `merch.notop`, `merch.nod`, `merch.nosec`) is written with its reason in the Data issues list; no "–" in the table is left without a reason.
- **Expansion to per hectare:** plot × period units in the confirmed tree list in which no stem at all was measured are also listed (their trees counted as not measured, no volume given; the volume of a plot with no trees at all is 0).
- **Interface:** section boundaries in the profile plot; in Excel the top solid by its translated name, thresholds without floating-point residue (7, not 7.000000000000001); an explanatory message if the Excel library cannot be loaded; the phrase "at this stage" in the interface was removed.
- **Validation:** `tests/secvol/test_sv_fix.mjs` 20/20 (hand-computed frustum and cylinder totals, Heger 1965 Figure 1: η = .333, .577, .745, .882, 1 → λ₀.₉ = .555; hand-written cases for `plotTotals` and `plotAreas`); `test_sv01` and `test_sv03` passed unchanged; `tests/secvol/e2e.mjs` 65/65.

## 15. Site Index (`assets/lab/siteindex/calc.js`; TOOLS_SPEC §3)

### SI01: dynamic equation core (12 Nov 2026)
**Source:** Hernández-Cuevas, M., Santiago-García, W., De los Santos-Posadas, H.M., Martínez-Antúnez, P., Ruiz-Aquino, F. 2018. Modelos de crecimiento en altura dominante e índices de sitio para *Pinus ayacahuite* Ehren. *Agrociencia* 52: 437–453 **[B]** (open-access PDF read; Cuadro 2 base models, Cuadro 3 ADA forms). The original sources cited from there: Bailey, R.L., Clutter, J.L. 1974. Base-age invariant polymorphic site curves. *Forest Science* 20: 155–159 **[B]** (PDF provided by the user, read on 22 Nov 2026: eq. 2, log H = a_i + b(1/A)^c with a site-specific a_i, eliminated through the base age in eq. 3; with c = 1 the Schumacher form ln H₂ = ln H₁ + b₁(1/A₂ − 1/A₁) used here); not read **[D]**: Richards, F.J. 1959 (Chapman–Richards); Schumacher, F.X. 1939. A new growth curve and its application to timber-yield studies. *Journal of Forestry* 37: 819–820 (original not obtainable (22 Nov 2026, user decision; see the legend)). The GADA forms of Cieszewski and Bailey (2000) are **not present** at this stage (source not read).

Dynamic (algebraic difference, ADA) equations: one parameter of the base model is site-specific and is eliminated from the observed (A₁, H₁); the equation passes through this point and is independent of the base age. Site index SI = f(A₀, A, H), the top height at the base age. Age in years, height in m.

| Id | Base model | Difference equation | Site-specific | Type |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `cr.ada.b0` | H = b₀(1 − e^(−b₁A))^b₂ | H₂ = H₁·[(1 − e^(−b₁A₂))/(1 − e^(−b₁A₁))]^b₂ | b₀ | anamorphic |
| `cr.ada.b2` | same | H₂ = b₀·(H₁/b₀)^[ln(1 − e^(−b₁A₂))/ln(1 − e^(−b₁A₁))] | b₂ | polymorphic |
| `schu.ada` | ln H = b₀ + b₁/A | ln H₂ = ln H₁ + b₁(1/A₂ − 1/A₁) | b₀ | anamorphic |

- The source's polymorphic b₂ notation H₂ = b₀[1 − e^(−b₁A₂)]^(ln(H₁/b₀)/ln(1 − e^(−b₁A₁))) is algebraically identical to the one in this table (b₀·g₂^(ln(H₁/b₀)/ln g₁) = b₀·(H₁/b₀)^(ln g₂/ln g₁)).
- **Schumacher sign warning:** the source uses the notation H = b₀·exp(−b₁/E) (b₁ estimate positive). Since the form ln H = b₀ + b₁/A is used here, a growing curve requires **b₁ < 0**; if b₁ ≥ 0 is entered no result is given and the reason is written (`par.b1neg`): a source coefficient in the form exp(−b₁/A) must be entered as −b₁.
- **Domain:** b₁ > 0 and b₂ > 0 (Chapman–Richards; at b₁ ≤ 0, 1 − e^(−b₁A) ≤ 0), b₀ > 0 (polymorphic), A > 0, H > 0; in the polymorphic form the asymptote of all curves is b₀, so no curve passes through a point with H ≥ b₀ (`h.aboveasym`). A projection whose result is not a positive finite number (`result.bad`) is not given either. At invalid ages the curve point is null.
- ln(1 − e^(−x)) is computed with log(−expm1(−x)) for x < ln 2 and with log1p(−e^(−x)) above it (no cancellation loss at small and large x).
- The **age type** (total / breast height) and the **base age** are written to the `settings` field of every result; the tool does not convert age types into each other (K8).
- The forms are registered in the shared coefficient record (`model-coef/1`, §13) with the ids `si.cr.ada.b0`, `si.cr.ada.b2`, `si.schu.ada`, with their global parameters only; the site-specific parameter is not entered.

**Validation:** `tests/ref/make_siteindex_si01.py` (SciPy 1.18.1): without using the difference equation, the base model is solved from the observation for the site-specific parameter with `brentq`, and height, SI and curve are computed from the base model (300 cases for each form, realistic parameter ranges; not real data). `tests/siteindex/test_si01.mjs` 3686/3686: projection, SI, site-specific parameter and curves 10⁻¹¹ (relative) against the reference; for each form, in 1000 random cases, consistency, path independence, base-age invariance (SI₁₀₀ = f(100, 50, SI₅₀)) and every point of a curve giving the same SI within 10⁻¹² (TOOLS_SPEC §8.2); anamorphic curves being proportional, polymorphic ones not; all undefined cases giving a status code instead of a number.

### SI02: site index computation with a coefficient set and interface (12 Nov 2026)
Page `site-index.php` (Tool 9, category "Stand and site", version 1.0.0-beta.1), interface `assets/lab/siteindex/ui.js`. Steps: Model, Data, Results.
- **Coefficient set** (`model-coef/1`, shared editor): form, global coefficients, **age type** and **top height definition** (required; support for tool-specific "extra fields" was added to the editor, `set.meta`), optional validity range (age, top height) and source reference (required). There is no built-in set (K1). The editor now reports all missing items in a record together (previously, if an extra field was missing, the source check was not shown).
- **Base age:** a page setting, with no default (the one of the classification used is entered). Since the difference equations are independent of the base age, the base age does not change the curves, only the age at which the index is read (stated on screen).
- **Classes** (`classesOf`, `classify`): none, equal-interval (start, width, 1–20 classes) or increasing bounds typed by the user; [lower, upper) intervals, the last one includes its upper bound; names I, II, … (I the highest or the lowest site index, the user chooses). An index outside them is "outside the classes", it is not forced into the nearest class. The bounds of no yield table are embedded.
- **Input:** a single point; a table (plot, period, age, top height; decimal setting; unreadable or empty cell "not computed"); confirmed tree data: units `buildUnits` (unmeasured combinations excluded), H0 with `unitMetrics(u).dom`, i.e. with the functions of the Sample Plot tool (the 100 thickest or tallest trees per hectare, fractional k, only measured heights; if H0 cannot be given the point is not computed), age = mean of the trees with recorded age. **The age type in the data** is selected; if it differs from the age type of the equation, no computation is done (K8).
- Points outside the **validity range** are computed and flagged with a warning (`evaluate`, TOOLS_SPEC §1.1).
- **Outputs:** table (unit, age, top height and its source, SI_A₀, class, notes, status); curve bundle (indices at the class bounds; without classes the minimum, median and maximum of the computed indices) and observation points; in every result the equation, base age, age type, top height definition and "produced with user coefficients: <source>". Excel (Info: coefficients, source, settings; Site index; Curves), CSV, JSON.
- **Interface fix (general):** labels and select boxes inside `.lims` cannot overflow the container (long form names were widening the page on phones); `lab.css` version 25.
- **Document fix:** in the contract table of §6 the return value of `buildUnits` was written wrongly (not an array but `{ units, periods, noPeriod }`); corrected.
- **Validation:** `tests/siteindex/e2e.mjs` 38/38: no built-in set; a set is not saved without age type, top height definition and source; no progress without a base age; SI on screen identical to the engine; no computation if the data age type differs; when a class bound changes, the class changes and SI does not; at base age 100, SI = the projection of SI₅₀ to age 100; table input (decimal comma, unreadable cell, validity range warning); for confirmed data, H0 and age identical to the functions of the Sample Plot tool, no H0 when selecting by height while there are unmeasured heights; CSV/JSON/Excel; English; phone.

### SI03: fitting to own data (12 Nov 2026)
- **Data** (`seriesFromTable`): age–height series (a tree in stem analysis, the top height of a plot in repeated measurement); the series id is text, and if a plot is given it is added to the id. Unreadable or non-positive age/height, empty id and repeated age within a series are reported with their row numbers, not corrected.
- **Pairing** (`makePairs`): `consecutive` (1→2, 2→3, …), `first` (1→2, 1→3, …), `all` (every i < j); only forward in age, equal ages do not form a pair. The choice is a setting and changes the result (Section 3.3); it is written in the result and the record.
- **Fitting** (`fitPairs`): least squares on H₂ = f(A₂, A₁, H₁), `core/nls.js` Levenberg–Marquardt, **analytical gradients** (`GRAD`; d/db₁ ln(1 − e^(−b₁A)) = A/expm1(b₁A); in the polymorphic form q = ln(H₁/b₀), r = L₂/L₁: ∂H₂/∂b₀ = e^(qr)(1 − r), ∂H₂/∂b₁ = b₀e^(qr)·q·(L₂′L₁ − L₂L₁′)/L₁²); stopping xtol 10⁻¹⁵, ftol 10⁻¹⁶, gtol 10⁻¹², at most 1000 iterations (written in the result). The solver does not step to parameters that violate the form's rules (`valid`); a non-converging, diverging or rule-violating result cannot be saved to the record.
- **Starting values:** entered by the user or the best of a small grid (tool convention: Chapman–Richards b₁ ∈ {0.005, …, 0.08}, b₂ ∈ {0.8, …, 2.5}, in the polymorphic form b₀ ∈ {1.1, …, 3}·largest height, Schumacher b₁ ∈ {−5, …, −90}); the start used and its origin are written in the result.
- **Criteria:** e = observed − predicted; bias ē, RMSE = √(SSE/n) (the same as Model Performance, §7), σ̂ = √(SSE/(n − p)); standard errors from σ̂²(JᵀJ)⁻¹ and labelled **"assuming independent observations"** (the observations of the same series are not independent; series-level bootstrap SI04).
- **Saving to the record:** origin `fitted`, fit summary (data name, number of pairs and series, pairing, method, start, iterations, RMSE, bias, σ̂, standard errors and their note), age type and top height definition (required), source reference (required; written by the user), validity range = the age and height range of the data.
- **Interface:** in the Model step, the collapsible card "Fit coefficients to my own data": table, columns, decimal setting, form, pairing, start; in the result, coefficients and standard errors, criteria, the curves of the series (each passing through the oldest observation of its series) and the residual–age plot.
- **Validation:** `tests/ref/make_siteindex_si03.py` (SciPy 1.18.1): for each form 25 simulated series (known global parameters, site-specific parameter per series, multiplicative noise; not real data), pairs by separately written code, `least_squares` ('lm', finite-difference Jacobian) from the same fixed start. `tests/siteindex/test_si03.mjs` 166/166: number of pairs for three forms × three pairings (from the definition and from the reference), coefficients 10⁻⁶ (relative), SSE, RMSE, bias, standard errors (10⁻⁴; SciPy uses a numerical Jacobian), the grid start reaching the same optimum, the coefficients returning within 10% of the generating values, identity of the analytical gradients with central differences within 10⁻⁶, statuses and table reading. `tests/siteindex/e2e.mjs` 51/51 (coefficients on screen with two pairings 10⁻⁷ against SciPy, RMSE, plots, rejection of a missing reference, origin, summary and selection of the saved set).

### SI04: uncertainty (12 Nov 2026)
- **Series-level bootstrap** (`bootstrapSeries`, fitted sets): **series** are resampled with replacement (not individual observations, since they are not independent); the resampling order from a single mulberry32 stream, replicate after replicate, in each replicate k draws ⌊r·k⌋ (`resampleIndices`; `core/stats.js` `rng`). Pairs are rebuilt with the same pairing (if the same series is drawn twice it counts as two separate series) and the equation is refitted starting from the estimate on the full data. Interval: the **percentile interval** of the successful replicates (type 7), default 95%; bootstrap standard deviation (n − 1); the number and rate of failed replicates, B and the seed in the result and the record. Defaults B = 200, seed 20261112 (tool conventions). The coefficients of the replicates (`fit.bootstrap.thetas`) are written to the saved set; in the results the 95% interval for the SI of each point is computed from these replicates (`siIntervals`).
- **Delta method** (`deltaSI`, sets entered by the user): if the source gives the covariance matrix of the global coefficients (optional field in the editor, rows separated by ";"), Var(SI) ≈ gᵀΣg, g = ∂SI/∂θ (analytical gradient of the difference equation, A₂ = A₀); with the normal approximation SI ± z·se. Matrix format, positive diagonal, symmetry and positive semidefiniteness (Cholesky) are checked; if not satisfied no interval is given and the reason is written. If there is no covariance, **"no uncertainty information"** is written; the "assuming independence" standard errors of the fit are not used to produce an interval.
- **Version** 1.0.0-beta.3 (bootstrap and the delta method were added to the method set, K4).
- **Interface fixes:** when a saved set is selected, the previous result is invalidated and the step bar is redrawn after the library is refreshed (previously, with a new set selected, steps 2 and 3 could remain locked and the result step could show the old computation).
- **Validation:** `tests/ref/make_siteindex_si04.py`: the same seed and the same resampling order (the Python counterpart of mulberry32, the same as in `make_hd_reference.py`), pairs with separate code, each replicate with `least_squares`, percentiles `numpy.quantile(method='linear')`. `tests/siteindex/test_si04.mjs` 91/91: for three forms (with three separate pairings) the resampled series identical, the coefficients of each replicate within 10⁻⁶, percentile intervals and SI intervals 10⁻⁶, identical replicates with the same seed, different with another seed; counting of failures; in the delta method the gradient against central differences 10⁻⁶, se = √(gᵀΣg), interval ± 1.95996·se; covariance checks. `tests/siteindex/e2e.mjs` 62/62 (bootstrap intervals on screen 10⁻⁷ against Python, the replicates of the saved set, bootstrap and delta intervals in the results, "no uncertainty information" for a set without covariance).

### Audit fixes (12 Nov 2026; version stayed 1.0.0-beta.3, computations unchanged)
- **Age type (K8):** for sets loaded from JSON too, the required extra fields (age type, top height definition) are checked as in the editor; if missing, the set is not loaded (`core/coef-ui.js`). If an old set without an age type is selected, no computation is done and the reason is written; the raw key (`x.ageType.undefined`) does not appear on screen.
- **Confirmed data mode:** Data Checker 1.1.0 maps tree age (§5); the mode now works with the data coming from the Checker.
- **Confidence interval:** two numeric columns (lower, upper) in the result table and in Excel/CSV; not text.
- **Interface:** in the Model step the two paths were separated into tabs ("Coefficient set from a source" / "Fit to my own data"); the bootstrap runs in a Web Worker (same function and seed, same result; on the page if there is no Worker); simulated sample data for fitting (`tests/ref/make_siteindex_sample.py`; the coefficients do not belong to any species or region); record errors and the Excel loading error with translated messages; the page introduction mentions fitting and uncertainty.
- **Deliberately not changed:** the base age is stored with the page setting, not with the set (a deviation documented in SI02); in the delta method the positive semidefiniteness check allows a margin of 10⁻¹² of the diagonal (for rounded covariance values in the source).
- **Validation:** `tests/siteindex/test_si_classes.mjs` 15/15 (classes: equal width and bounds, half-open interval, last class closed, I highest/lowest, rejection of invalid definitions; validity range warnings); `tests/siteindex/e2e.mjs` 64/64.

**R code (21 Nov 2026):** the fit (SI03) can be downloaded as an R script (`assets/lab/siteindex/rcode.js`): the same series and pairs, the chosen difference equation fitted by `nls` from the tool's starting values (Gauss–Newton, fallbacks: the tool's estimates, then the PORT algorithm, each said in the output). Checked in R by `scripts/rcode-check.sh` (`tests/rcode/siteindex.mjs`: three cases, the three forms and pairings, one with a plot column, decimal comma and rows left out; parameters, standard errors, n, SSE, RMSE, bias, σ agree with the tool).

## 16. Stand Structure (`assets/lab/structure/calc.js`; TOOLS_SPEC §4)

### SS01: non-spatial structure and density (12 Nov 2026)
The unit, the expansion factor (10000/A; only for fixed-area plots), N/ha, G/ha, Dq, H0 and the diameter classes are functions of the Sample Plot and Stand tool (`buildUnits`, `unitMetrics`, `classesFromBreaks`, `breaksFromWidth`, `classIndex`; the §6 contract); they are imported, not rewritten. The weight of each tree is w_i = the expansion factor (equal within a plot); the functions were written with general weights and tested as such. No page yet (SS04). Result record `structure-result/1`: status (`ok`, `empty`, `invalid`), issues and warnings as codes, settings and the definition of each measure.

| Measure | Definition | Source |
|---|---|---|
| Diameter mean, standard deviation, CV, skewness, kurtosis | central moments with divisor W = Σw (n with equal weights): s = √m₂, CV = s/x̄, g₁ = m₃/m₂^1.5, kurtosis **excess** g₂ = m₄/m₂² − 3; if all diameters are equal, skewness and kurtosis are undefined (null, warning) | statistical definition |
| Gini | G = Σᵢ Σⱼ wᵢwⱼ |xᵢ − xⱼ| / (2W²x̄), all ordered pairs (including i = j); with ascending order G = Σᵢ wᵢxᵢ(W₍<ᵢ₎ − W₍>ᵢ₎)/(W·Σwx); x is basal area g (default) or diameter (separate option, separate label). 0: all equal; at most 1 − 1/n | statistical definition |
| Shannon (diameter) | H′ = −Σ pₖ ln pₖ, pₖ the share of trees per hectare; classes by start and width (default 0 and 4 cm, tool convention), last class open; trees below the start are counted with a warning | statistical definition |
| Species mixture | species shares (by number), Shannon H′, Simpson 1 − Σp² (Gini–Simpson) and Σp² (dominance) under separate names; warning if species is empty | statistical definition |
| BAL | BALᵢ = Σ_{j: dⱼ > dᵢ} wⱼgⱼ (m²/ha); equal diameters excluded (default) or included (setting); never the tree itself | definition |
| Relative size | dᵢ/Dq and gᵢ/ḡ (= (dᵢ/Dq)²) | definition |
| SDI | SDI = N·(Dq/D_ref)^β, β = 1.605, D_ref = 25 cm (both settings) | Reineke, L.H. 1933. Perfecting a stand-density index for even-aged forests. *J. Agric. Res.* 46(7): 627–638 **[B]** (USDA Treesearch 60134, public domain; read 12 Nov 2026): log N = −1.605 log D + k, D the mean diameter by basal area (Dq), N trees/acre; SDI is the number of trees at which the line parallel to the reference curve crosses the ordinate of **10 inches**. The slope 1.605 is from the source; the metric 25 cm reference diameter is a **tool convention** (Reineke himself used 10 inches = 25.4 cm; selectable in the settings, a factor of (25.4/25)^1.605 ≈ 1.026 difference). |
| Curtis relative density | RD = G/Dq^b, G m²/ha, Dq cm; b = 0.5 default (setting) | Curtis, R.O. 1982. A simple index of stand density for Douglas-fir. *Forest Science* 28(1): 92–94 **[B]** (USDA Treesearch 7202, public domain; read 12 Nov 2026): RD = G/Dg^b (Equation 5); b = 0.5 is the rounded value for coastal Douglas-fir (Equation 7); Reineke's −1.605 slope corresponds to b = 0.40 and "may be used in the absence of better information" (source). The default b = 0.5 is from the specification (TOOLS_SPEC §4.1); it is species-specific and written in the result. |
| Relative spacing (Hart; Hart–Becking) | S% = 100·a/H0; for square planting a = √(10000/N), for triangular planting a = √(2·10000/(N√3)); planting pattern a setting and in the result; H0 by the Sample Plot definition (the 100 tallest trees per hectare; until 19 Nov 2026 the 100 thickest by default, selectable); if there is no H0, S% is not given | **[D], definition exception** (12 Nov 2026, user decision): Hart (1928) and Becking (1953) could not be read; secondary sources give the name "Hart–Becking" sometimes to triangular, sometimes to square planting. The formula is a geometric definition; instead of the name, the planting pattern is selected explicitly. |

**Validation:** `tests/ref/make_structure_ss01.py` (NumPy, SciPy; separate from the JS): weighted moments with NumPy, unweighted with `scipy.stats.skew`/`kurtosis(bias=True)`; Gini with a double loop over all pairs (the JS uses the sorted form); Shannon `scipy.stats.entropy`; BAL brute-force double loop (equals excluded and included); on simulated plots (3 plots × 2 periods, unequal areas, species, heights; not real measurements) N/ha, G/ha, Dq, H0 (recomputed in Python), class and species shares and all measures. `tests/structure/test_ss01.mjs` 172/172: TOOLS_SPEC §8.3 (SDI 559.177149815208; S% 17.677669529664 square, 18.995892141290 triangular), unequally weighted and tied samples, Gini limits (0 and 1 − 1/n), exact identity with the Sample Plot values in the units, settings and definitions written to the record, rejection of an invalid setting, empty plot (no measure, not zero), no S% without H0.

### SS02: spatial structure (12 Nov 2026)
`assets/lab/structure/spatial.js` (DOM-free). **Source status (16 Nov 2026; sources in the user's Drive folder, MODUL_KAYNAK):**
- Clark, P.J., Evans, F.C. 1954. *Ecology* 35: 445–453. **[B]**: R = r̄_A/r̄_E, r̄_E = 1/(2√ρ), upper limit 2.1491 for a hexagonal pattern (Appendix); for the edge, "the nearest neighbour is measured if it is outside the area, a tree outside the area does not become a centre" (the counterpart of the buffer mode). The five examples in Table II (r̄_E and R) are in the test; in the scanned text the Liatris r̄_A reads "8.0537", the value consistent with R·r̄_E = 3.0537 was used.
- Pommerening, A. 2002. *Forestry* 75: 305–324. **[B]**: M_i = (1/n)Σv_ij (1 if the species differs; in the paper n = 3), T_ij = 1 − min/max (the reference tree and its j-th neighbour; the paper gives T1, T2, T3 separately), W_i = (1/n)Σw_ij (1 if the angle α is smaller than α₀; **α₀ = 360°/n, 90° with 4 neighbours**; the smaller of the two angles of each neighbour pair). U and Donnelly are not in this paper; in the paper no edge correction is applied to the indices.
- **Divergences and decisions (16 Nov 2026, user):** the W standard angle became an option: default 360°/k (Pommerening 2002, [B]); 360°/(k + 1) (72° for k = 4; the later definition of Hui and Gadow, the default of forestSAS; source not read, [D], original not obtainable (22 Nov 2026, user decision; see the legend)). T remains the mean over k neighbours (tool convention); the source's T1 (with the nearest neighbour) is given in addition. The number of neighbours k is shared by all indices (default 4; the paper uses 3 for M and T).
- **U [B] (22 Nov 2026):** Hui, G.Y., Albert, M., von Gadow, K. 1998. Das Umgebungsmaß als Parameter zur Nachbildung von Bestandesstrukturen. *Forstwissenschaftliches Centralblatt* 117: 258–266 (PDF provided by the user, read on 22 Nov 2026: eq. 1, Uᵢ = (1/n) Σ kᵢⱼ, kᵢⱼ = 0 if neighbour j is smaller than the reference tree i, otherwise 1; Figure 1: "größer oder gleich", so equal diameters count 1, as in the tool; the paper uses n = 3, the tool k, default 4). **Earlier, U and Donnelly [P] (18 Nov 2026):** U (Hui et al. 1998; forestSAS 2.0.5 `dominance` documentation: "the proportion of the 4 nearest neighbours that are larger than the reference tree") and the Donnelly (1978) expectation (spatstat.explore 3.8.3 `clarkevans(correction = "Donnelly")`). The original sources were not read. With `tests/ref/ss02_check.R` run by the user (R 4.6.2; output `tests/ref/structure_ss02_r.json`) and `tests/structure/test_ss02_r.mjs`: 3 patterns, 4 nearest neighbours for 233 trees, U (each tree exactly) and uncorrected R agreed. **Donnelly constant:** the tool used 0.041 (the value in the other secondary sources citing Donnelly); spatstat uses 0.0412 (back-calculated on two rectangular patterns), the difference in R_D is about 2·10⁻⁵ relative. **User decision (18 Nov 2026): spatstat is followed, 0.0412** [P]; the original text (Donnelly 1978, Hodder (ed.) *Simulation Methods in Archaeology*, pp. 91–95): original not obtainable (22 Nov 2026, user decision; see the legend). The comparison of Hegyi with siplab was not written to the output in the first run (in siplab 1.6 the column name is `cindex`, not `cmpt`); the script was fixed to take the last column and the user reran it (18 Nov 2026): **Hegyi (6 m radius, no edge correction) identical within 1e−9 to siplab 1.6 `pairwise(powers_ker)` for 150 trees on two rectangular patterns**; the Hegyi [A] status is supported by this numerical validation. `test_ss02_r` 12/12.
- **Plot geometry** (from the user, m): circle (centre, radius) or axis-parallel rectangle. Every tree without coordinates or outside the area becomes an issue with its row number and the unit is not computed (an incomplete map spoils all indices); if there are no coordinates at all, the status is "no coordinates" (not zero). Warning if the area of the geometry differs from the recorded area by more than 1%. Two trees at the same location are warned with their two rows.
- **Neighbours:** the k nearest neighbours by Euclidean distance (default k = 4, setting; at equal distance input order, tool convention), with growing rings on a regular grid (10 000 trees about 0.2 s).
- **Clark–Evans:** R = r̄_A/r̄_E, r̄_A the mean distance to the nearest neighbour, r̄_E = 0.5/√λ, λ = n/A (trees/m²). **Donnelly:** r̄_E,D = 0.5·√(A/n) + (0.0514 + 0.0412/√n)·P/n, P the perimeter (0.0412 as in spatstat, user decision 18 Nov 2026; earlier versions 0.041); since the Donnelly expectation is itself an edge correction, R_D is always computed with the plain nearest-neighbour distances of all trees (no buffer or translation applied).
- **W (uniform angle):** W_i = (1/k)·Σ v_j; the neighbours are sorted by azimuth, each of the k angles between consecutive neighbours is the smaller angle (≤ 180°); v_j = 1 if the angle < α₀ = 360°/(k + 1) (k = 4: 72°). **M (mingling):** 1 if the neighbour's species differs. **T (diameter differentiation):** 1 − min(d_i, d_j)/max(d_i, d_j). **U (dominance):** 0 if the neighbour is thinner than the tree, otherwise 1 (equal diameter counts as 1). Unit values are the mean over the reference trees.
- **Edge effect** (written in every result): "no correction" (all trees are reference trees; a tree closer to the boundary than to its k-th neighbour is flagged and counted as an "edge tree"); "buffer" (trees closer than b m to the boundary are only neighbours; default b = 5 m tool convention; no computation if no reference tree remains, warning if fewer than half remain); "toroidal translation" (rectangle only; distances to the nearest copy). In buffered R, λ is from the whole area.
- **Validation:** `tests/ref/make_structure_ss02.py` (`scipy.spatial.cKDTree`, also with a periodic box; azimuths with NumPy; separate from the JS). `tests/structure/test_ss02.mjs` 70/70: TOOLS_SPEC §8.3 (on a square grid R = 2 and with k = 4 W = 0, on a hexagonal grid R = 2.149139863647; toroidal); on random patterns (rectangle and circle) neighbour ids, nearest-neighbour and boundary distances, per-tree W, M, T, U, edge trees, the reference set in the buffer, R and R_D, toroidal neighbours and means; Poisson simulation (400 patterns × 50 trees, 30 × 30 m): uncorrected mean R 1.070 (larger than 1 due to the edge effect), 1.005 with Donnelly; issue cases; timing on 10 000 trees.

### SS-07/a1: competition indices (12 Nov 2026; TOOLS_SPEC SS03)
`assets/lab/structure/competition.js` (DOM-free). Id scheme `docs/PLATFORM_PLAN.md` P3. **Source status: Hegyi (1974) [A]** (16 Nov 2026): cited by Contreras, M.A., Affleck, D., Chung, W. 2011. Evaluating tree competition indices as predictors of basal area increment in western Montana forests. *Forest Ecology and Management* 262: 1939–1949, Table 1, CI8 = Σ dᵢ/(d·distᵢ) (fixed-radius neighbourhood of 11 m) **[B]**; second independent source the `kernel` documentation of the siplab 1.6 R package: (S_j^p_j/S_i^p_i)/R^p_r, for Hegyi p_i = p_j = p_r = 1 **[P]**. Divergence (condition 4): according to the siplab documentation, the original Hegyi adds 1 ft (0.3048 m) to the distance; the two secondary sources give the form without the addition, the tool uses the form without the addition and states this on screen and in the guide. Numerical check: `tests/ref/ss02_check.R` (siplab `pairwise`, 6 m). Original reference **[İ]** (bibliography of Contreras et al.).
- **Non-spatial:** BAL and relative size (d/Dq, g/ḡ) are taken unchanged from `structureUnit` of SS01.
- **Hegyi:** CIᵢ = Σⱼ (dⱼ/dᵢ)/dist_ij; competitors are either all trees within radius R (R the user's choice, **no default**) or the nearest k trees (default k = 4, the same as SS02, tool convention). If there is no competitor within R, CI = 0 and their number is written with a warning.
- **Edge effect** the same as SS02: "no correction" (an index for every tree; trees whose competitor search extends outside the area, i.e. those closer to the boundary than R or than their k-th competitor, are flagged); "buffer" (trees in the buffer are not given an index but are competitors of others); "toroidal" (rectangle only, nearest copy; in radius mode 2R must be smaller than the short side, otherwise a competitor would be counted twice, and the computation is rejected).
- **Same location:** two trees with dist = 0 are written as an issue with their two rows; neither is given an index (no infinite value), and they continue to be competitors of the other trees. Trees without coordinates or outside the area are issues with their row numbers as in SS02.
- **Columns added to the tree table:** `appendColumns` adds columns without changing the source rows (the scheme of the Sample Plot tool); an existing name gets "_ci".
- **Neighbour search:** in radius mode a grid with cell side R. In the toroidal case the side is divided into whole cells. The same fix was also applied to the k-neighbour search of SS02: when the last cell was narrow, a neighbour wrapping around from the edge could be missed.
- **Validation:** `tests/ref/make_structure_ss07.py` brute-force double loop over all pairs (no neighbour search; nearest copy in the toroidal case). `tests/structure/test_ss07.mjs` 25/25: in rectangle and circle 4 and 6 nearest, R = 6 m (with the number of competitors), edge trees, 5 m buffer, toroidal (radius and k); hand computation (20 cm tree, competitors of 30 cm at 2 m and 10 cm at 4 m: 0.875); tree without competitors; same location; rejection of radius mode without R; in the unit BAL and d/Dq identical to SS01; without geometry only the non-spatial measures; added columns; timing on 10 000 trees.

### Page: Stand Structure and Competition (12 Nov 2026; TOOLS_SPEC SS04; 1.0.0-beta.1)
`stand-structure.php`, `assets/lab/structure/ui.js` (interface), `run.js` (DOM-free: all units at once), `i18n.js`. Ids SS-02, SS-04, SS-05, SS-06, SS-07.
- **Data:** tree table (shared import; plot, diameter, plot size required; tree, species, height, period, x, y optional), confirmed data or simulated sample data (`tests/ref/make_structure_sample.py`: three circular plots of 500 m², 158 trees; near-regular, clustered and random; species codes are placeholders). Coordinates were added as optional x, y to the shared `tableToDataset` function of the Sample Plot tool (m; negative and zero valid; only one of the two is an issue with its row number); if no coordinate column is selected, the records are as before.
- **Units:** `buildUnits(ds, { emptyCombos: 'unmeasured' })`: in data with periods, plot–period combinations without tree records are deemed not measured, are not computed, and their number is written.
- **Geometry:** circle, centre (x, y) and radius √(A/π) from the recorded area (default, tool convention); circle, values entered; rectangle, corners entered. The coordinates are in the same frame on each plot. If the spatial computation cannot be done on a plot (outside the area, missing coordinates), structure and density are still given.
- **Lorenz curve:** with ascending order (cumulative share of trees, cumulative share of x), x the same as in Gini (basal area or diameter); the identity Gini = 1 − 2·(area under the curve) is checked in the test with the trapezoidal rule.
- **Stem map:** circles proportional to diameter, enlarged 3 times (drawing only); colour by the tertiles of the Hegyi values on the plot.
- **Export:** Excel (Info: tool, version, settings, geometry, definitions, source status; Plots; Trees: source rows as read + BAL, d/Dq, g/ḡ, Hegyi, edge, W, M, T, U; Classes; Species), tree table + competition columns, CSV, JSON.
- **Validation:** `tests/structure/test_ss04.mjs` 27/27 (coordinate reading; in each unit the structure, spatial and Hegyi values identical to the respective functions; Lorenz endpoints and the Gini identity; geometry options; data without coordinates; unmeasured combinations; tree outside the area). `tests/structure/e2e.mjs` 35/35 (sidebar and ids; sample data; the 15 measures in the table identical to the engine; detail plots; BAL and Hegyi in the tree table; buffer and radius setting; Excel sheets and added columns; "–" for data without coordinates; English; phone and dark theme).

### SS-02: crown competition factor, CCF (17 Nov 2026; `structure/ccf.js`; Stand Structure 1.0.0-beta.3)
Sources: Kershaw et al. 2016, §8.9.4 (pp. 264–265) [B] and van Laar and Akça 2007, pp. 156–157 [B], both citing Krajicek, Brinkman and Gingrich (1961) (the original paper could not be found, not read). The two sources give the same definition; the difference in scale is shown deliberately.
- **Definition:** for open-grown trees without competition, the relation between crown width and diameter at breast height CW = b₀ + b₁d (least squares; Kershaw step 2); the maximum crown area of a tree as a percentage of unit area MCA = 100·(πCW²/4)/A (Kershaw eq. 8.26; in SI 0.0078·CW², A = 10 000 m²); CCF = Σ nᵢ·MCAᵢ (nᵢ the number of trees per unit area; eq. 8.27). At CCF = 100, crowns of open-grown size would cover the ground fully; CCF is not a measure of crown closure (Krajicek et al., cited by Kershaw). van Laar and Akça: MCA = b₀ + b₁d + b₂d² (m²), CCF = Σ MCA/10 000 (a ratio, 1/100 of Kershaw's value). The tool gives the percentage (Kershaw, Krajicek) and writes the ratio (van Laar) as a separate column in the export.
- **Coefficients** (K1): in the coefficient library tool `ccf`, two forms: `ccf.cw` CW (m) = b₀ + b₁d and `ccf.mca` MCA (m²) = b₀ + b₁d + b₂d²; d cm, outside bark. One set for all species or sets by species (the species' set first). On the page, a CW–d line is fitted from open-grown trees (n ≥ 3; R², residual standard error) and, once its reference is entered, saved as an `origin: fitted` set; the validity range is the diameter range in the sample.
- **Computation:** for each plot × period, weight wᵢ = the plot's per-hectare multiplier (the Sample Plot and Stand definition); CCF = Σ wᵢ·100·MCAᵢ/10 000. On a measured plot without trees CCF = 0. If a species has no set, or if CW or MCA is not positive for a tree, the CCF of that plot is not given and the reason is written: a partial total would understate the density (tool convention). Trees outside the validity range are counted and enter the computation.
- **Not implemented:** Krajicek's imperial-unit coefficients or other built-in MCA equations (K1); special rules for combining MCA across species (not in the sources; each tree with the set of its own species).
- **Validation:** `tests/ref/make_ccf_ss02.py` (mpmath 40 digits: 40 stands, three species, two forms; with a single set additionally Kershaw's expanded eq. 8.27; 25 lines with numpy.polyfit); `tests/structure/test_ccf.mjs` 138/138: CCF and each MCA 1e−11, SI constant 0.00785, the constant 0.0018·CW² in Krajicek's imperial example, CCF = 100 at full cover, no CCF with a missing species or negative crown width, a set without a source is not used, the plot's per-hectare multiplier; `tests/structure/e2e.mjs` 42/42 (line fitting, rejection of a record without a reference, saving as a set, CCF column identical to the engine, MCA in Excel).

## 17. Taper (`assets/lab/taper/calc.js`; TOOLS_SPEC §6; PLATFORM_PLAN SV-02, SV-03)

### SV-02/a1: profile core, Max and Burkhart (1976) (16 Nov 2026; TOOLS_SPEC TP01)
Source: Max, T.A., Burkhart, H.E. 1976. Segmented polynomial regression applied to taper equations. *Forest Science* 22: 283–289 **[B]** (read from the user's Drive folder). Form `taper.mb76` = **Model 4** of the paper (quadratic–quadratic–quadratic; Gallant and Fuller 1973 reparameterisation):
- y = b₁(x − 1) + b₂(x² − 1) + b₃(a₁ − x)²·I₁ + b₄(a₂ − x)²·I₂; I_i = 1 if a_i − x ≥ 0; y = d²/D², x = h/H. In the paper d is the diameter inside bark, D the DBH outside bark, H the total height (ground to tip), h the height above ground; the model is constrained so that d = 0 at x = 1. In the scanned text the exponents of the Model 4 equation read garbled; the form was verified by the parameter count: three quadratics (9 coefficients) − continuity and first derivative at the two join points (4) − y = 0 at the tip (1) = 4 β + 2 join points = the 6 parameters in Table 3. The Table 3 coefficients (loblolly pine, inside bark) are used only in the test; they are not embedded in the site (K1).
- Coefficient set: `model-coef/1`, `tool: 'taper'`, `form: 'taper.mb76'`, parameters b1, b2, b3, b4, a1, a2; **`output.bark` required** (ob or ib: which diameter the set gives); inputs D (cm, outside bark), H (m), h (m). a₁, a₂ ∈ (0, 1) (relative-height join points in the paper), otherwise an issue.
- d(h) = D·√y; h < 0 or h > H is "outside"; if y < 0 then "negative" (the coefficients give no real diameter there; no number is shown).
- Volume V(h₁, h₂) = (π/40000)·D²·H·[Y(x₂) − Y(x₁)] m³, Y(x) = b₁(x²/2 − x) + b₂(x³/3 − x) + b₃[a₁³ − (a₁ − min(x, a₁))³]/3 + b₄[a₂³ − (a₂ − min(x, a₂))³]/3 (closed form). If the interval contains a region with y < 0, no volume is given (the quadratic of each piece is examined exactly). For comparison, Gauss–Legendre (20 nodes) ∫ d² dh split at the join points.
- Height of a given diameter: **all** roots in [0, H] of D²y(h/H) − d*² = 0: a 2000-interval grid + join points, sign changes refined with Brent (xtol 1e−13 m). Multiple roots (butt swell or a profile increasing at the base) are all written with the status "multiple". Known limitation: a tangent (double) root that does not change sign may be missed.
- Consistency (on every tree): d(H) (0 by construction in mb76), d(1.3) − D and d(1.3)/D (not corrected; in an inside-bark set it is smaller by the bark), regions with y < 0 (height intervals, warning); a tree outside the set's validity range (`valid.D`, `valid.H`) is computed and warned about.
- **No bark model:** no sourced bark model form has been read (the "bark model implementation" in TP01 is deferred until a source arrives); outside-bark and inside-bark profiles are two separate coefficient sets (`output.bark`).
- **Verification:** `tests/ref/make_taper_tp01.py` (equation written separately from the paper; volume `scipy.integrate.quad` with the join points as break points, epsrel 1e−13; height by a 20 000-interval scan + `brentq`; the two sets of Table 3, four copies of them perturbed by ±15% (two with y < 0 regions, not realistic) and an artificial set increasing at the base; random trees). `tests/taper/test_tp01.mjs` 745/745: 252 diameters (1e−12), 72 volumes closed form = quad and = Gauss–Legendre (worst relative 1.6e−15; acceptance 1e−10), no volume on 33 intervals with y < 0, 108 height searches (root count and values 1e−9 m; including multiple roots), d(h(d)) = d (worst 1.6e−14; acceptance 1e−10), record and set checks.

### SV-02/a2: Kozak (2004) Model 02 (16 Nov 2026)
Source: Kozak, A. 2004. My last words on taper equations. *The Forestry Chronicle* 80: 507–515 **[B]** (Drive). Form `taper.kozak02` = Model 02 of the paper (equation 4; overall the best model across 38 species groups in the paper's comparison):
- d = a₀·D^a₁·H^a₂·X^E, E = b₁z⁴ + b₂·(1/e^(D/H)) + b₃X^0.1 + b₄·(1/D) + b₅H^Q + b₆X; X = [1 − z^(1/3)] / [1 − p^(1/3)], Q = 1 − z^(1/3), z = h/H, p = 1.3/H. In the paper d is the diameter inside bark (cm), D the DBH outside bark (cm), H the total height (m), h the height above ground (m). In the scanned text the equations are garbled; all terms of Model 02 (z⁴, 1/e^(D/H), X^0.1, 1/D, H^Q, X; H^a₂; the 1/3 exponent of X and Q; p = 1.3/H) were made out from the text. Model 01 (equation 3) was not added because the first term in its exponent was lost in the scan; Models 88 and 94 can be added separately with the Kozak (1988, 1997) sources.
- The paper has no coefficient table; the tool ships no coefficients (K1). The paper estimates the coefficients by logarithmic linearisation and, where needed, with the Baskerville correction (to be noted in the TP02 fitting).
- Properties: X(1.3 m) = 1, i.e. d(1.3) = a₀·D^a₁·H^a₂ (in the test); at the tip X = 0; if the tip exponent E(1) = b₁ + b₂e^(−D/H) + b₄/D + b₅ > 0 then d = 0; if ≤ 0 there is no diameter at the tip ("not a number", warning `tip.nonfinite`; the height is not searched up to the tip: no root is searched in the last grid interval because d goes to infinity there, and the Python reference skips the same interval). y = d²/D² cannot be negative.
- Volume: no closed form; **adaptive Gauss–Legendre** (10 and 20 nodes compared per piece; an absolute error budget of 1e−14 times the first estimate over the whole interval is shared among the pieces in proportion to their widths; at most 60 subdivision levels and 20 000 pieces). The slope is infinite at the tip (X^0.1); the absolute budget keeps the subdivision finite (with a relative budget the subdivision did not stop, as seen during testing).
