## 24. Volume equations (`assets/lab/volume/calc.js`; PLATFORM_PLAN SV-04, TOOLS_SPEC SV04; 17 Nov 2026, 1.0.0-beta.1)
User decisions (17 Nov 2026): a separate page (`volume-equations.php`); forms combined variable, logarithmic, constant form factor and local; goodness-of-fit evaluation via transfer to Model Evaluation.

Source **[B]**: Kershaw, J.A., Ducey, M.J., Beers, T.W., Husch, B. 2016. *Forest Mensuration*, 5th edition. Wiley, §6.2 (pp. 156–159; part 2 uploaded by the user, verified from the page images):
- §6.2.1 standard functions: (6.22) constant form factor V = b₁D²H; (6.23) combined variable V = b₀ + b₁D²H; (6.24) V = b₁D^b₂H^b₃ and its logarithmic transformation (6.25). **The book printed the left side of (6.25) as "V =";** the logarithmic transformation of (6.24) is ln V = ln b₁ + b₂ ln D + b₃ ln H (footnote: base-10 logarithms may also be used; the tool uses natural logarithms). In the tool (6.24) is fitted only in this logarithmic form (no nonlinear fitting).
- §6.2.2 local functions: (6.29) V = b₀D^b₁ and the linearised ln V = ln b₀ + b₁ ln D; V = b₀ + b₁D² cited from Prodan.
- D diameter at breast height (cm), H total height (m), V volume (m³); the coefficients depend on these units and are written in the set.

Implementation:
- All forms by linear least squares (logarithmic forms on the ln scale); standard errors s²(XᵀWX)⁻¹, s² = Σw·e²/(n − p).
- **Weight** (only forms on the volume scale; chosen by the user): σ²_t ∝ X_t^k, X = D²H (6.22, 6.23) or D² (local); k is given or is the slope of the regression ln e² = a₁ + k ln X from the least-squares residuals (Parresol 1999 Equations 12, 15 [B], the same as BC-02/a1). In the weighted form s is the standard error of the weighted residuals.
- **Back-transformation** (logarithmic forms): none or Baskerville exp(s²/2), s the residual standard error on the ln scale ([A], §22). In the record `transform: { kind: 'log', correction: 'baskerville', value: s }`; `core/coef.js` `correctionFactor` gives the same factor.
- **Goodness-of-fit criteria** on the volume scale, with the Model Evaluation tool's own `metrics` function (§7; e = observed − predicted; bias, MAE, RMSE, R² = 1 − SSE/SST). Observed and predicted volumes are transferred to Model Evaluation with the key `handoff:model-degerlendirme` (role: training; that page reads it once and deletes it).
- Data: table (D, H, V and the bark side of the volume) or the volumes transferred by Sectional Volume (BC03 `forest-derived/1`, row by row with the D and H of the active package; outside or inside bark). Trees with missing/invalid values are excluded with their rows.
- Record: `model-coef/1`, `tool: 'volume'`, forms `volume.cf`, `volume.cv`, `volume.ln`, `volume.loc`, `volume.locln`; `origin: 'fitted'`, source description required, validity range the D (and H) range of the sample, in `fit` the method, n, coefficients, standard errors, covariance, weight and criteria; the bark side of the volume in `meta.bark`.
- Out of scope: equations with a form variable (6.27, 6.28), Honer (6.26), upper-diameter-limit volume ratios (§6.2.3), nonlinear fitting, mixed-effects models.

Test: `node tests/volume/test_sv04.mjs` (23; `tests/ref/make_volume_sv04.py` standard library only, written separately: five forms, unweighted/estimated k/given k, Baskerville; coefficients, standard errors, s, k, factor and criteria), browser `node tests/volume/e2e.mjs` (21; values on screen the same as the engine, record, transfer to Model Evaluation, Sectional Volume volumes, phone).

## 25. Log scaling (`assets/lab/logscale/calc.js`; PLATFORM_PLAN SV-05; 17 Nov 2026, 1.0.0-beta.1)
User decisions (17 Nov 2026): board-foot rules are also implemented (the result is labelled as board feet; an exception to P5 for this tool), alongside metric cubic volume; a separate page (`log-scaling.php`).

Source **[B]**: Kershaw, J.A., Ducey, M.J., Beers, T.W., Husch, B. 2016. *Forest Mensuration*, 5th edition. Wiley (pp. 160–200 set aside by the user; formulas verified from the page images):
- §7.3 (pp. 186–191): board foot, lumber 1 inch thick; D the inside-bark diameter at the small end (in), L the log length (ft). Scribner (Bruce and Schumacher 1950 regression) V = (0.79D² − 2D − 4)·L/16 (7.3); Doyle V = ((D − 4)/4)²·L (7.5); International ⅛ inch (Clark 1906) for 8, 12, 16, 20 ft V = 0.44D² − 1.20D − 0.30; 0.66D² − 1.47D − 0.79; 0.88D² − 1.52D − 1.36; 1.10D² − 1.35D − 1.90 (7.7–7.10), a log longer than 20 ft is scaled as two or more logs; International ¼ inch for every length (Grosenbaugh 1952a, least squares) V = 0.0497621LD² + 0.006220L²D − 0.185476LD + 0.000259L³ − 0.011592L² + 0.042222L. Clark's tables are rounded to 5 board feet (optional).
- §7.4.1 (p. 195): diameter inside bark, at the small end, to the nearest inch; a log exceeding the maximum scaling length is scaled as two or more logs, if it does not divide evenly the longer piece is at the butt; the scaling diameter at the division point is estimated from the taper of the log; the trim allowance is not included in the scaling length.
- Table 6.1 (p. 141): Smalian V = L/2·(A_b + A_u) (6.5), Huber V = A_m·L (6.6), Newton V = L/6·(A_b + 4A_m + A_u) (6.9).

Implementation:
- Measurements are entered in cm and m; for the board-foot rules D_in = D_cm/2.54, L_ft = L_m/0.3048 (exact). Scaling convention choices (written in the result): diameter to the nearest inch (default on; §7.4.1), scaling length down to whole or even feet (default whole; with a 1e−9 tolerance) or unrounded; optional maximum scaling length (default none; the book's 16 ft east, 40 ft west are examples).
- Division: k = ⌈L/L_max⌉ pieces, whole feet, as equal as possible, the extra feet go to the butt pieces; the small-end diameter of the butt pieces is linear between the small and large end (tool rule; the book only says "estimated from the taper") and requires the large-end diameter.
- The rules are applied piece by piece and summed; a length without an International ⅛ inch equation, D ≤ 4 in for Doyle and a non-positive value have the status "not computed". If there is a log that cannot be computed with a rule, the total for that rule is not given.
- Cubic volume from the measured metric values, unrounded: Smalian if there is a large end, Huber if there is a mid-diameter, Newton if there are both.
- Out of scope: defect deduction (the five rules in §7.4.1, net volume), Spaulding (7.4), Maine and combination rules, country-specific metric log standards.

Test: `node tests/logscale/test_sv05.mjs` (13; the book's Table 7.1: for 16 ft logs Doyle exactly at nine diameters, the Grosenbaugh equation rounded to 5 board feet gives all nine values of the International ¼ inch column; the Scribner column only against the reference, since it is Scribner's original unsmoothed table (7.3); `tests/ref/make_logscale_sv05.py` standard library only, 40 logs: scaling length, pieces, four rules, Smalian/Huber/Newton), browser `node tests/logscale/e2e.mjs` (12; 20 in × 16 ft Doyle = 256 board feet).

## 26. Nonlinear regression (`assets/lab/nlsreg/calc.js`, `assets/lab/core/expr.js`; PLATFORM_PLAN SM-02; 17 Nov 2026, 1.0.0-beta.1)

User decisions (17 Nov 2026): ready-made forms and free formula together; weighted NLS, confidence/prediction intervals, model comparison, saving to the coefficient library; a separate page (`nonlinear-regression.php`). The free-formula decision lifts, for this tool, the rule in TOOLS_SPEC §1.3 that "free formula entry (expression parser) is not in this package"; the form registry of the other tools does not change.

**Source [B]:** Parresol, B.R. 2001. Additivity of nonlinear biomass equations. *Canadian Journal of Forest Research* 31: 865–878 (read in §22 BC-02/a2): model with additive error y = f(x, β) + ε (Equation 1); variance function σ²_t = σ²·ψ_t (Equations 2–3); θ by OLS of ln e²_t = ln σ² + θ·ln X_t from the unweighted least-squares residuals (Equations 4–5); weighted (EGNLS) solution S(β) = [y − f]ᵀΨ⁻¹[y − f] (Equation 7), cov(b) = σ̂²[ZᵀΨ⁻¹Z]⁻¹, σ̂² = S(b)/(T − K) (Equations 9a–b); coefficient intervals in a way similar to linear least squares (b ± t·SE); for the mean zᵀs²(b)z (Equation 10), for a new observation σ̂²ψ + zᵀs²(b)z (Equation 11). Estimation algorithm: Levenberg–Marquardt, `core/nls.js` (definitions in §10; checked against SciPy).
- **Ready-made forms:** Chapman–Richards [B], Schumacher [B], Gompertz [A], logistic [A], monomolecular [A], Weibull [A] (the forms and reading statuses in §18; variable x instead of t) and the power forms y = a·x^b, y = a·x1^b·x2^c (Parresol 2001 Equation 15 [B]). Starting values (tool rule): for growth forms the grid in §18 (five points with the lowest SSE), for power forms ln–ln OLS; the user can also enter them.
- **Free formula (tool rule, `core/expr.js`):** recursive descent parser, no `eval`. Grammar: + − * / ^, parentheses, exp, ln, log10, sqrt, abs, pi; ^ is right-associative and binds tighter than unary minus (−x^2 = −(x²), 2^3^2 = 512); "log" is rejected because it is ambiguous; · × − are accepted; at most 500 characters, 60 nesting levels, 12 coefficients. Derivatives are computed exactly in forward mode (dual numbers); for a constant exponent the derivative of u^w is w·u^(w−1)·u' (no ln for a negative base). Every name not mapped to a column is a coefficient; a name identical to a column header is mapped to the column. The canonical text of the formula (with the fewest parentheses) gives the form id: `nls.expr.<FNV-1a(text|variables|coefficients)>`.
- **Weight:** none; ψ = X^θ (X a column; θ is given or estimated with Equation 4; not estimated if the unweighted fit does not converge); ψ = the value of a column (positive). The fit is on y/√ψ = f/√ψ.
- **Common rows (tool rule):** all models are fitted to the rows in which the dependent variable and every column used by any model are numbers (positive in weight columns); the others are listed with row and column. In this way AIC and the criteria are compared on the same data.
- **Criteria:** RMSE, bias, R² with the definitions of the Model Evaluation tool (§7; e = y − ŷ), on the y scale and unweighted. AIC (tool rule): Gaussian likelihood with variance σ²ψ_t, σ² at its maximum likelihood value (S/n): ln L = −n/2·ln(2πS/n) − ½Σ ln ψ_t − n/2, AIC = 2k − 2 ln L, k = number of coefficients + 1 (an estimated θ is not counted; written on screen). At ψ = 1 it is the same as the AIC in §18.
- **Statuses:** converged, did not converge (not hidden), covariance singular, could not be fitted (n ≤ p + 1, no/undefined start, invalid weight). Student t, n − p df, 95%.
- **Record:** `model-coef/1`, tool `nls`, form `nls.<ready-made id>` or `nls.expr.<hash>`; inputs with the variable names in the formula, units written by the user (required); in `fit` the formula text, variables, SE, covariance, σ̂², df, weight, AIC, criteria; validity range the data range of each variable. The Coefficient Library re-registers the form of formula sets from the formula text in the set (also when restoring from a backup); a set whose text does not match its id is not saved.
- **Out of scope:** constrained parameters, mixed effects (SM-03), systems of equations (BC-02, SM-04), autocorrelated errors, profile likelihood and bootstrap intervals.

Test: `node tests/nlsreg/test_sm02.mjs` (41): parser (precedence, rejections, canonical text, exact derivatives against central differences); Parresol 2001 Table 2 (the 40 slash pines in Table 1; bole as the formula a*(D^2*H)^b, bark and crown with the ready-made power forms, crown weight as the column D^7.322·e^(−0.006185·H²)): unweighted and weighted coefficients, SE and σ̂² to the printed digits (unweighted crown 1e-4 relative), θ = 2.114 and 3.715 (Equation 4), Equation 10 example 186.4 kg and 8.46 kg², Equation 11 example 304.94 kg²; independent reference `tests/ref/make_nlsreg_sm02.py` (NumPy, model functions written by hand, Jacobian by central differences): five fits on the page's simulated table (Chapman–Richards, formula with +1.3, logistic, weighted power with estimated θ, column-weighted power), coefficients, SE, σ̂², AIC, criteria, t, intervals. `tests/nlsreg/e2e.mjs` (18).

## 27. Mixed-effects models (`assets/lab/mixed/calc.js`; PLATFORM_PLAN SM-03; 17 Nov 2026, 1.0.0-beta.1)

User decisions (17 Nov 2026): linear and nonlinear mixed models together; calibration (new group), variance/covariance structures, model comparison, saving to the library; sources and R output from the user. Page `mixed-models.php`.

**Sources [B]:** Pinheiro, J.C., Bates, D.M. 2000. *Mixed-Effects Models in S and S-PLUS*. Springer (full text uploaded by the user, 17 Nov 2026; §2.2 Equations 2.4–2.23, §2.4.1, §5.2, §7.2 Equations 7.10–7.19 read). Lindstrom, M.J., Bates, D.M. 1990. Nonlinear mixed effects models for repeated measures data. *Biometrics* 46: 673–687. **Calibration [A]:** the Vonesh and Chinchilli (1997) approximate Bayes estimator, cited in Raptis, D.I. et al. 2021, *Trees* 35: 1167–1183 and Ciceu, A. et al. 2020, *For. Ecol. Manage.* 477: 118507 (two independent secondary sources; numerical verification with R). **Numerical reference [P]:** R package nlme 3.1-168 (R 4.5.2), `tests/ref/sm03_mixed.R`, run by the user (17 Nov 2026), output `tests/ref/mixed_sm03.json`; package code and data were not used (data: the page's simulated table).
- **Linear mixed model (LME):** y_i = X_iβ + Z_ib_i + ε_i, b_i ~ N(0, Ψ), ε_i ~ N(0, σ²Λ_i). Marginal form (2.14): y_i ~ N(X_iβ, σ²Σ_i), Σ_i = Λ_i + Z_iΨ̃Z_iᵀ, Ψ̃ = Ψ/σ². β̂(θ) by generalised least squares; σ̂² = rᵀΣ⁻¹r/N (ML) or /(N − p) (REML). ℓ_ML = −N/2·log(2πσ̂²) − N/2 − ½Σlog|Σ_i|; ℓ_REML = −(N − p)/2·log(2πσ̂²) − (N − p)/2 − ½Σlog|Σ_i| − ½log|ΣX_iᵀΣ_i⁻¹X_i| (2.23; with the identities Σlog(|Δ|/|R11(i)|) = −½Σlog|Σ_i| and |R00|² = |ΣXᵀΣ⁻¹X|). θ: log standard deviations of Ψ̃ in the diagonal form, log-Cholesky parameters in the general form (§2.2.7) and δ in varPower; the profile likelihood is maximised by BFGS with central-difference gradients (tool rule; the parameterisation affects only the optimisation). cov(β̂) = σ̂²(ΣXᵀΣ⁻¹X)⁻¹. Random effect prediction (BLUP) b̂_i = Ψ̃Z_iᵀΣ_i⁻¹(y_i − X_iβ̂).
- **Variance function (§5.2, varPower):** Var(ε_ij) = σ²|v_ij|^(2δ), v a variable of the model (tool rule: so that its value is known in calibration).
- **Nonlinear mixed model (Lindstrom–Bates):** φ_i = β + b_i (in the selected coefficients); the PNLS step, with Δ fixed, minimises Σ[‖y_i − f_i(β, b_i)‖²_Λ + b_iᵀΨ̃⁻¹b_i] with respect to β and all b_i (7.10; Gauss–Newton with step halving); the LME step maximises with respect to θ the LME likelihood (7.12, 7.13 for REML) of the working response ŵ_i = y_i − f_i + X̂_iβ̂ + Ẑ_ib̂_i, X̂ = ∂f/∂βᵀ, Ẑ = ∂f/∂bᵀ (7.11); iterated until the change in θ and β becomes small. The reported log L, AIC, BIC and cov(β̂) are those of the last LME step (as in nlme). Start: nonlinear least squares on all the data (SM-02 engine), the diagonal of Ψ̃ on the scale of 10% of the coefficients (tool rule).
- **AIC, BIC, LRT:** AIC = −2ℓ + 2k, BIC = −2ℓ + k·ln N (ln(N − p) for REML, as in nlme), k = fixed effects + covariance parameters + variance parameters + 1. Likelihood ratio L = 2(ℓ₁ − ℓ₀), χ² (difference in k); conservative for a variance component tested on the boundary (§2.4.1); only between two ML fits.
- **Calibration (new group):** one step b̂ ≈ DẐᵀ(R̂ + ẐDẐᵀ)⁻¹ê, Ẑ = ∂f/∂b and ê = y − f(β̂, 0) with the random effects at zero, R̂ = σ̂²Λ [A]; in a linear model this is the exact BLUP. Option (tool rule): conditional mode, the same step iterated at the current b (β̂, D, σ̂ fixed; the single-group form of PNLS).
- **Record:** `model-coef/1`, tool `mixed`, form `mixed.<FNV-1a(formula|variables|coefficients)>` (the fixed part as a formula; in a linear model e.g. b0 + b1*ln(D)); coefficients the fixed effects; `meta`: coefficients with random effects, D, σ, δ; `fit`: method, formula, covariance, SE, cov(β̂), log L, k, AIC, BIC. The Coefficient Library re-registers the form from the set's formula.
- **Discrepancy from the source/reference (note):** nlme's REML nonlinear fit (N1r, started from the ML estimates) stayed at the relative precision of the ML fit: σ² and D exactly N/(N − p) times the ML values, β that of ML. The tool's 7.13 formula reproduces nlme's log L (−596.019772) exactly at nlme's estimates; in the same linearisation the REML optimum is higher, and the tool finds D = 14.118 at −596.0093. nlme's fit with two random effects and a general covariance (N3) gave a "false convergence" warning in the LME step; nevertheless the results agree with the tool to the digits shown.
- **Out of scope:** several nested group levels, autocorrelated errors (§5.3), generalised mixed models, Laplace/Gaussian quadrature approximations (§7.2), variance functions other than varPower.

Test: `node tests/mixed/test_sm03.mjs` (31): seven LME fits against nlme (intercept only, intercept and slope general and diagonal, varPower; REML and ML): β, SE, σ, D, δ, log L, k, AIC, BIC, BLUPs, fitted values; LRT (anova.lme); new-plot calibration (BLUP); four NLME fits (ML; on a, on a and b diagonal and general, varPower): β, SE, σ, D, δ, log L, AIC, BIC, random effects; LRT (anova.nlme); REML formula and the discrepancy; one-step and iterated calibration. Additional check (during development, not a permanent test): the LME log Ls also agree with statsmodels 0.15 MixedLM to 1e-6. `tests/mixed/e2e.mjs` (15).

## 28. Systems of equations (`assets/lab/eqsys/calc.js`; PLATFORM_PLAN SM-04; 17 Nov 2026, 1.0.0-beta.1; 19 Nov 2026, 1.0.0-beta.2: identifiability and LR reliability)

User decisions (17 Nov 2026): general SUR/NSUR with the user's formulas (cross-equation restriction by a shared coefficient name), verification with R systemfit, a separate page (`equation-systems.php`). Additive biomass systems stay on the Biomass and Carbon page (BC-02).

**Sources [B]:** Henningsen, A., Hamann, J.D. 2007. systemfit: a package for estimating systems of simultaneous equations in R. *Journal of Statistical Software* 23(4) (the open-access vignette version of systemfit 1.1-30 on CRAN, read on 17 Nov 2026; §2.1, §2.4–2.7, Equations 1–8, 37–39, 42–43, 48). Nonlinear equations: Parresol, B.R. 2001, *Can. J. For. Res.* 31: 865–878, Equations 20–26 [B] (§22). **Numerical reference [P]:** systemfit 1.1-30 (R 4.5.2), `tests/ref/sm04_systems.R`, run by the user (17 Nov 2026), output `tests/ref/eqsys_sm04.json`.
- **Model:** G equations, each with T observations; y_i = f_i(X, β) + u_i, E[uuᵀ] = Σ ⊗ I_T (Equations 1–6). Formulas with `core/expr.js` (SM-02); every name not mapped to a column is a coefficient, a name occurring in more than one equation is a single coefficient (cross-equation equality restriction). A row with a missing value in one equation is removed from the whole system.
- **Estimation:** β̂ = argmin uᵀ(Σ̂⁻¹ ⊗ I)u, Gauss–Newton with step halving (Parresol 2001 Equation 24; for linear equations the solution of Equation 7 in a single step); cov(β̂) = (FᵀΩ̂⁻¹F)⁻¹ (Equation 8). **OLS:** estimation with Ω̂ = I; covariance with σ̂ᵢ² per equation, or, if there is a shared coefficient, with a single pooled σ̂² = ΣSSR/(GT − K) (§2.5; systemfit's default, checked numerically). **SUR:** Σ̂ from the first-step (restricted) OLS residuals; **iterated SUR:** Σ̂ from the previous residuals at each step, stops when the relative change of the coefficients √(Σ(Δβ)²/Σβ²) < 10⁻¹² (tool rule; different from systemfit's stopping rule, same result).
- **Residual covariance:** σ̂_ij = ûᵢᵀûⱼ/T (Equation 37), /√((T − Kᵢ)(T − Kⱼ)) (Equation 38, default) or /(T − max(Kᵢ, Kⱼ)) (Equation 39); Kᵢ the number of coefficients in the equation (shared coefficients are counted in each equation).
- **Fit:** R²ᵢ = 1 − ûᵢᵀûᵢ/(yᵢ − ȳᵢ)ᵀ(yᵢ − ȳᵢ) (Equation 42); system OLS R² = 1 − ΣSSR/ΣSST (systemfit's `ols.r.squared`); McElroy R² (Equation 43, with the Σ̂ used in estimation). log L = −GT/2·log(2π) − T/2·log|Σ̂₃₇| − GT/2, df K + 1 for OLS, K + G(G + 1)/2 for SUR (as in systemfit, checked numerically). Likelihood ratio T(log|Σ̂_r| − log|Σ̂_u|) (Equation 48): in the interface "Test of the shared coefficients"; the unrestricted system is built by separating each shared coefficient per equation (a → a_1, a_2), both systems are fitted by iterated SUR with Equation 37 (maximum likelihood, §2.4), the unrestricted one is started from the restricted estimates; χ², df the number of added coefficients (user decision, 17 Nov 2026).
- **Record:** each equation a separate `model-coef/1` set (tool `eqsys`, form `eqsys.<FNV-1a(formula|variables|coefficients)>`), with the shared coefficients and the covariance block, with the same `fit.system` id; cross-equation coefficient covariances are not written to the set.
- **Discrepancy from the reference (note):** systemfit's `nlsystemfit` function did not move away from the OLS estimates in the SUR step on these data (SUR = OLS; nlm optimiser); the OLS estimates are the same as the tool's (≤ 10⁻³ SE). Nonlinear SUR and iterated SUR are therefore checked against a separately written NumPy reference (`tests/ref/make_eqsys_sm04.py`).
- **Identifiability and reliability of the LR test (tool rules, 19 Nov 2026, user decision; 1.0.0-beta.2):** the information matrix A = Fᵀ(Σ̂⁻¹ ⊗ I)F is scaled to a unit diagonal (C = D⁻¹AD⁻¹, D = diag √Aᵢᵢ) and the condition number λmax/λmin (symmetric Jacobi eigenvalues) is computed; if it exceeds **10⁸** (the threshold in the Fonseca recovery) the fit is considered "weakly identified" and the coefficients with |component| ≥ 0.3 in the eigenvector of the smallest eigenvalue are written as the non-identifiable combination (warning on the main fit; coefficients whose standard error is larger than the estimate are noted separately). The LR test of the shared coefficients gives a p-value only under these conditions: (1) the unrestricted log L is not smaller than the restricted one (relative 10⁻⁸); (2) none of the separated copies has |value| > 10⁶·max(1, |shared value|); (3) the condition number of the unrestricted fit ≤ 10⁸; (4) when the unrestricted system is fitted from four more starts, from the restricted estimates and with the second and later separated copies multiplied by 0.5, 0.8, 1.25, 2, all reach the same maximum (relative 10⁻⁶; the other starts are run only if the first fit passes 1–3; in a non-identifiable system they are slow and unstable). If one does not hold, the status is "not reliable": the reasons and, for information, the LR are given, no p-value is given. Rationale: in the compatible height–volume system in the sample data, the unrestricted a₂, b₂, c₂, q cannot be identified from the data (condition number ~10¹⁴, c₂ runs off to ~10¹⁶), and different starts gave different log L between −76.5 and −71.6; 1.0.0-beta.1 put one of these into the LR test without a warning.
- **Out of scope:** 2SLS/3SLS (endogenous regressors; not chosen by the user), weighted systems, linear restrictions other than equality, different T per equation.

Test: `node tests/eqsys/test_sm04.mjs` (32): eight linear fits against systemfit (OLS, SUR with three residual covariances, iterated SUR with two definitions, OLS and SUR with shared coefficients): coefficients, SE, residual covariance, R², system OLS R², log L and df (one-step ≤ 10⁻⁹, iterated ≤ 10⁻⁶ relative); LRT (lrtest) with the engine function and with testRestrictions, which builds the unrestricted system itself; McElroy R² (Python); nonlinear free and compatible systems: nlsystemfit OLS, OLS, SUR, iterated SUR against the NumPy reference (coefficients ≤ 10⁻⁴ SE, SE ≤ 10⁻⁴ relative, SSR ≤ 10⁻⁷). `tests/eqsys/e2e.mjs` (10).
